# VOLUME - STARGATE UNIVERSE

*Intergalactic void - stasis crossing*

---

# STARGATE UNIVERSE: TECHNICAL MANUAL

**DESTINY EXPEDITION SHIP'S REFERENCE, FIRST EDITION**

*Third volume of the set, companion to the SG-1 and Atlantis Expedition References. Prepared in the tradition of the Star Trek Writers'/DS9 Technical Manuals: an in-universe reference consolidating broadcast canon, with clearly marked engineering extrapolation.*

---

## ABOUT THIS DOCUMENT

This manual provides the detailed technical background that stories aboard the Ancient ship *Destiny* sometimes need. It is a supplement to, not a replacement for, character, and this volume needs that warning most of all, because *Destiny*'s stories are people stories. The ship is a decaying coffin with a mission; the drama is who you become inside it.

**Edition pin.** This First Edition reflects status quo as of **the end of the expedition's recorded operations: all hands in stasis, ship on automated FTL toward the Signal's source** (see §1.5). Events after that point are not reflected here.

**Conventions used throughout:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Established by on-screen canon as broadcast |
| **[E]** | Extrapolation — internally consistent engineering detail where the screen was silent. May be refined, never casually contradicted |
| **[W]** | Writer's latitude note — guidance on how hard this rule binds you |

Where this manual conflicts with a filmed episode, the episode wins. Log the conflict in Appendix C.

**Companion volumes:** Milky Way network doctrine (SG-1 Reference), Pegasus city systems and vessel specifications (Atlantis Reference).

**Structure:** Part One sets the theater between galaxies. Part Two is *Destiny* herself. Parts Three–Five cover gate operations, vessels, and equipment. Part Six is the threat dossier. Part Seven covers command legitimacy under strain. Part Eight catalogs phenomena. Appendices carry glossary, conversions, the canon log, and index.

---

## PART ONE: THE THEATER BETWEEN GALAXIES

### 1.0 Introduction

*Destiny* is fifty million years old, launched before humanity's ancestors had names, following a signal embedded in the cosmic microwave background toward an unknown destination. Her crew did not volunteer for her, they fled through her gate to survive an attack, and she jumped before anyone could leave. Every system in this manual should be read through that lens: no resupply, no rotation, no map home.

**In brief:** Where SGC builds and Atlantis inherits, the *Destiny* expedition *scavenges*. The ship predates scarcity as the Ancients experienced it; her crew live inside her decline.

### 1.1 The Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Intergalactic void, course unknown — billions of light-years from Earth and receding |
| Command post | The Ancient ship *Destiny*, under way except for gate-stop intervals |
| Complement | ~80 survivors of Icarus Base plus absorbed Lucian Alliance prisoners **[E]** |
| Supply state | No resupply of any kind; every consumable is finite |
| Contact with home | Consciousness-swap stones only; no matter, no ships |

Three facts define every decision aboard:

1. **The ship is dying faster than the crew can fix it.** *Destiny* has run fifty million years past her maintenance life. Every system works until it doesn't, and nothing is replaceable, only cannibalized.
2. **The mission is not theirs.** The Signal predates humanity. The crew can follow it, ignore it, or die arguing about it; they cannot change it.
3. **Everyone left behind a body on Earth.** Stone-swappers wake in borrowed flesh; everyone else's family thinks whatever Homeworld tells them. Grief at this distance is administrated.

**[W]** SGU stories are pressure-cooker stories: the enemy is rarely the point. Scarcity, legitimacy, faith, and boredom-at-knife-point are the engines.

### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Era | Event |
|---|---|
| ~50,000,000 years ago | Ancients launch seed ships across the cosmos, dropping stargates along planned routes; *Destiny* follows to study the Signal embedded in the cosmic microwave background — evidence of structure preceding the universe itself |
| Launch era → present | Ancients abandon the project over generations (Ascension, plague-era priorities). *Destiny* continues autonomously, seeding-gate to seeding-gate, decaying |
| 2009 CE | Icarus Project solves nine-chevron dialing via naquadria core resonance. Lucian Alliance attack destroys the base; survivors gate to *Destiny* mid-transit. Ship jumps immediately |
| Year 1 | Water crisis, air crisis, Lucian Alliance takeover attempt, first contact with the Nakai, discovery of the Bridge and Rush's master codes, Eden |
| Year 2 | Drone war escalates; Novus colony discovered (descendants of an alternate-timeline crew); Chloe's transformation resolved; command ship hub destroyed |
| End of record | Fleet engagement won at terrible cost; crew enters stasis; *Destiny* begins the long automated jump toward the Signal's origin **[W]** |

### 1.3 Assessment of Forces

*(Full dossiers in Part Six.)*

**The Expedition (~80 souls [E]).** A mixed bag of Icarus Base personnel, soldiers, scientists, civilians who were simply there when the base fell, plus a Lucian Alliance contingent absorbed after their takeover failed. No rotation exists. Command rests on Colonel Everett Young's shoulders and on everyone deciding, daily, to let it.

**The Drone Network.** Unmanned fighters coordinated by command ships, executing attack logic from a war whose principals are millions of years dead. They engage anything weapons-bearing; they do not negotiate because nothing is left to negotiate with. Their central hub's destruction slowed them but did not end them **[W]**.

**The Nakai.** A starfaring species competing for the same gates and resources, blue-skinned, telepathically gifted, technologically near-parity with Destiny. Withdrawn after heavy losses; withdrawal is not departure.

**Earth (distant).** Contactable only through Ancient communication stones swapping minds into bodies at Homeworld Command. Earth can send expertise, orders, and comfort; it cannot send food, fuel, or people. This asymmetry defines every conversation with home.

### 1.4 Founding Technologies

**In brief:** The expedition's toolkit is a fixed inheritance: one Ancient ship, her two shuttles, the kino fleet, communication stones to Earth, and whatever walked through the gate with eighty people on the worst day of their lives.

- **Communication stones:** paired devices swapping consciousness across any distance, the program's umbilical to Earth (§3.3).
- **Kinos:** floating spherical remotes, camera, intercom, scout, and the ship's de facto security system (§5.2).
- **The Ancient interface chair:** damaged; grants ship-system knowledge directly into the user's mind at risk of brain damage and worse (§2.2).
- **Salvaged weapons:** what the military carried plus Lucian Alliance arms after the takeover attempt (§5.1).

### 1.5 Status Quo Snapshot (Edition Pin: End of Recorded Operations)

| Domain | Status |
|---|---|
| Ship state | All hands in stasis; automated FTL under way toward the Signal's source — a crossing projected in years |
| Crew | ~80 in pods **[E]**; command succession pre-recorded for every contingency |
| Drone threat | Command-ship hub destroyed; residual fighters scattered but the class persists **[W]** |
| Nakai | Withdrawn after losses; intentions unknown |
| Earth link | Stones inactive during stasis crossing by design — waking bodies would starve without hands to feed them |
| The Signal | Unchanged. Waiting. |

**[W]** This pin is a suspended sentence, literally. Every post-pin story is a *waking*: something changed while they slept. That is the volume's standing invitation.

### 1.6 Icarus Base (Predecessor Installation)

**In brief:** The planetary research base whose destruction began the expedition: a fortified facility on an unnamed world, built around a naquadria core whose resonance signature could power a nine-chevron dial. The Lucian Alliance attack that cracked the core also forced the evacuation through *Destiny*, the attack and the discovery are the same event in the program's memory.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Purpose | Nine-chevron dialing research (Icarus Project) |
| Power plant | Planetary naquadria vein, tapped by Ancient-designed resonance equipment |
| Complement | ~100 military/civilian **[E]**; ~80 survived to the ship |
| Fate | Core detonation destroyed the planet's surface viability; site abandoned |

**Why it matters:** every person aboard was selected or present because of naquadria physics, not exploration fitness. The expedition is a lab cohort that became a crew. Icarus-class sites remain the only known key to nine-chevron addresses, which means somewhere, someone is drilling again **[W]**.

---

## PART TWO: DESTINY

### 2.0 Ship Overview

**In brief:** *Destiny* is the oldest known operating vessel in existence, an Ancient long-range exploration ship launched ahead of her support infrastructure, built to be self-sufficient across geological time and to be repaired by no one. Her design assumes crewed maintenance she has never had; her decay is fifty million years of that assumption failing gracefully.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Class | Ancient deep-space explorer (unique) |
| Launch era | ~50,000,000 years before present |
| Length | ~800 m **[E]** |
| Decks | 20+ sections in three hull zones **[E]** |
| Complement (designed) | Thousands **[E]**; current complement ~80 |
| Autonomy | Fully capable of unmanned operation; most systems run themselves until touched |
| Condition | Structurally sound; systems decaying non-uniformly — the ship's defining trait |

**Design language:** angular grey-green hull over an internal skeleton of corridors and shafts, lit sparsely by amber emergency lighting that is original equipment, not damage. Sections are designated by number; the bow hosts gate operations and the shuttle bays, the stern the FTL drive and engineering spine.

```
   DESTINY — PROFILE (schematic, not to scale)

    FRONT                                          STERN
   ____________
  /            \________________________
 | GATE ROOM   |  midship sections:     \______
 | [black gate] |  quarters · hydroponics       \_______
 | SHUTTLE BAYS |  infirmary · brig · labs              | FTL DRIVE
 |_____________|  kino dock · airlock                    | [star-scoop
        \        sealed/derelict sections ----####-------|  emitters]
         `---- bridge access (concealed) --------####-----'
   #### = sections lost to vacuum, sealed since arrival year
```

### 2.1 Structures and Zones

| Zone | Function | Condition |
|---|---|---|
| Bow / gate operations | The black stargate, kino dock, shuttle bays 1–3 (two shuttles survive **[E]**) | Active |
| Command deck ("the Bridge") | Master systems access — *concealed* until discovered mid-year-one; not on any crew-accessible map | Active, restricted |
| Midship habitat | Quarters, mess, hydroponics bay (crew-built from ship components) | Active |
| Medical bay | Ancient diagnostic beds; no regeneration fields — this ship predates them **[E]** | Active |
| Interface chamber | The damaged neural chair | Restricted |
| Engineering spine | FTL drive access, power distribution | Active, lethal when misused |
| Derelict sections | Vacuum-exposed, sealed; air and heat lost in the arrival year; some later reclaimed | Partial |

**[W]** Set geography: the gate room is the heart; the Bridge is the secret. Keep the walk between them long enough that controlling one is not controlling the other, the first season's politics live in exactly that gap.

### 2.2 Command Systems

**The Bridge.** Concealed behind a bulkhead requiring master codes; hosts the full-ship control interface. Its discovery mid-first-season restructured every power relationship aboard, whoever holds the Bridge holds the ship, and Rush held its keys alone for a time.

![Destiny control console](images/Stargate_Universe_Control_Console.jpg)
*Field reference: Ancient console interface, crystal keys, no readable labels until expedition overlays.*

**Master codes.** Rush's private override set, established via the interface chair. Command doctrine treats their existence as an open wound: legitimate command (Young) and technical access (Rush) remain permanently separate authorities. Every crisis since has been, at root, about whether those two things can be forced back together.

**The interface chair.** A neural knowledge-transfer device, damaged in transit, still functional at risk. It writes ship-system comprehension directly into a user's cortex. Known effects: total systems fluency; nosebleeds, seizures, personality drift with repeated use; one operator (Franklin) vanished entirely after a deep session, see §8.4. Use requires medical standing authorization and someone willing to say goodbye **[W]**.

### 2.3 Computer System

- Ship intelligence runs on distributed Lantean-era processing, largely autonomous, *Destiny* flies herself, repairs herself within limits, and gates ahead automatically.
- Human interfaces are limited: wall panels, kino feeds, the Bridge console. There is no general query language; the crew learned the ship like weather, by observation.
- The ship logs everything, including the crew. Privacy aboard *Destiny* is a social convention the hardware does not recognize **[W]**.

### 2.4 Power Generation

**In brief:** No fuel, no reactors to refuel: *Destiny* charges her capacitors directly from stars, diving shielded through stellar coronae on scheduled passes. Power state governs everything, FTL segments, life support margin, gate range, shield strength.

| Source | Notes |
|---|---|
| Star-scoop charging | Primary. Shielded corona dives recharge capacitors over hours; the maneuver is routine, spectacular, and never entirely safe — shields decayed mid-dive once, with casualties |
| Capacitor banks | Distributed through the engineering spine; degradation of individual banks produces the ship's characteristic brownouts |
| Auxiliary taps | Solar trickle while in normal space; negligible except for survival loads |

**Power discipline:** unlike every other volume in this set, there is no external supply line at all. A bank lost is a district dark forever; a star missed is an FTL delay measured in months. The expedition's calendar is written in stellar encounters.

### 2.5 Defensive Systems

**Shields.** Full-envelope Ancient shielding, powerful when charged, decaying with capacitor state. Fifty million years of micro-impacts have scarred the emitters; shield strength is the first thing power rationing touches and the last thing anyone wants it to touch.

**Armor and structure.** The hull has survived fifty millennia of dust at FTL speeds; against directed fire it performs like armor, not a forcefield. Sections sacrificed to vacuum are sealed, not mourned until later.

**Weapons.** Minimal by design: *Destiny* carries defensive batteries adequate to discourage fighters, nothing more. Her survival doctrine is flight, concealment (FTL signatures are hard to track), and repair, not engagement. The drone war was forced on her.

### 2.6 Propulsion

#### Faster-Than-Light Drive

**In brief:** *Destiny*'s FTL is not hyperspace. No subspace window, no gates, the ship translates directly across space in sustained acceleration segments that can run for days or years. It is faster than any hyperdrive known and effectively untrackable mid-segment.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Transit character | Continuous translation; no "window" events |
| Speed | Exceeds all known hyperdrive classes **[E]** |
| Tracking | Mid-flight signature extremely faint — the ship's best defense |
| Limitations | Course set at segment start; power-hungry; drive stress accumulates without maintenance windows |

**[W]** FTL's story property: once the jump begins, nobody gets off. Every plot that needs an exit ramps one up before the countdown or waits for arrival.

#### Maneuvering

Sublight via distributed thruster arrays; agility poor by design, she was built to travel in straight lines between stars for decades at a stretch.

### 2.7 Sensors and Communications

- **Long-range sensors:** detect gates, ships, and stellar bodies across interstellar ranges; resolution degrades with distance and age.
- **Subspace communication:** ship-to-gate-network only within local range; no direct Earth link exists, stones bridge that gap (§3.3).
- **Kino network:** the crew's own ad hoc sensor grid, see §5.2.
- **The Signal:** the ship continuously monitors the cosmic microwave background pattern that is her mission. The instrument array dedicated to it occupies her forward section and answers to no one **[W]**.

### 2.8 Environmental and Life Support

The arrival year nearly killed everyone twice: CO2 scrubber cascade failure, then water contamination. Fixes since: hydroponics bay (crew-built), ice-mining runs through the gate, scrubber components cannibalized from derelict sections, strict airlock discipline after the sabotage incidents. Water and air are rationed by policy and audited by kino, the most-read documents aboard are the consumables ledger and the duty roster.

### 2.9 Medical Facilities

An Ancient diagnostic suite reading full biometrics, plus whatever pharmaceuticals eighty people carried through the gate. No sarcophagus, no regeneration fields, no resupply: the infirmary's supply cabinet is a countdown. Psychological casualties outnumber physical ones roughly three to one **[E]**, treat accordingly.

### 2.10 Emergency Operations

| Condition | Trigger | Response |
|---|---|---|
| Section breach | Micrometeorite, combat, decay | Auto-seal; evacuation through kino-mapped routes |
| Power emergency | Capacitor bank failure | District brownouts; life support priority locked |
| Hostile boarding | Drone/Nakai/Lucian Alliance | Marine response + kino surveillance net; airlock denial |
| Stasis contingency | Uninhabitable ship | Full crew to stasis pods; automated recovery protocols pre-staged — executed at the edition pin |

---

## PART THREE: GATE OPERATIONS

### 3.1 The Black Gate

**In brief:** *Destiny*'s stargate predates the Milky Way and Pegasus networks' common design: a black, unlit ring with a fixed glyph set, no DHD, and dialing handled entirely by the ship's own systems.

- **Auto-dial doctrine:** the ship gates to whatever world lies along her course when she drops from FTL; the window is short and scheduled by stellar encounter, not by human convenience. Missed windows mean waiting for the next planet.
- **No nine-chevron lockout:** Destiny's gate connects only within the seeded local network, the seed ships dropped gates along the route specifically so the mission could step ahead. Dialing backward toward known space is impossible without Icarus-class power.
- **Gate travel remains one-way, 38-minute limited** (companion SG-1 §3.1 applies for fundamentals).

### 3.2 Off-World Operations

Every gate run is a supply run: water, food, fuel-grade materials, or nothing. Doctrine (hard-won):

1. Kino through first, always.
2. Armed overwatch at the event horizon until recall.
3. Hard time-limit discipline: the ship will leave. She has left people behind. Both facts are canon and neither is negotiable.
4. Anything brought aboard quarantines through medical before joining general stores.

### 3.3 Communication Stones

**In brief:** Paired devices swapping consciousness between two bodies across any distance, the expedition's only link to Earth. A swapper's mind rides a willing participant's body at Homeworld Command while that person's mind occupies their body aboard *Destiny*.

Operational rules:

- Bodies must be maintained: sleeping, feeding, exercising the vacated flesh is someone's duty roster line.
- Identity discipline: stone-swapped personnel carry Earth authority into the ship's politics, Telford's dual role made him simultaneously liaison, spy, and hostage of both sides.
- The stones have been used for interrogation, courtship, court martial testimony, and last goodbyes. They are the most ethically loaded objects in three volumes **[W]**.

### 3.4 Kino Operations

The kino fleet doubles as the gate-recon drone: thrown through first, hovering, mapping, listening. Standard survey package per stop: atmosphere, life signs, resources, threats, recorded and archived automatically to the ship's logs. Losing a kino costs nothing but pride; they are the cheapest things aboard.

---

## PART FOUR: VESSELS

**Recognition reference:** see plate `Stargate Universe - Ship Recognition Plate.png` (companion file, this folder).

### 4.1 Ancient Vessels

#### Seed Ship (derelict)

**In brief:** One of the automated ships that preceded *Destiny*, seeding stargates along the mission corridor. Found derelict with a single surviving drone-class guardian; her gate stock explained the network's existence.

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Length | ~500 m **[E]** |
| Complement | None (automated) |
| Role | Gate deployment, forward logistics |
| Status | Derelict; cannibalized for parts |

#### Shuttles

*Destiny* carries two surviving shuttlecraft (a third was lost **[E]**): small, shielded, FTL-*incapable*, system-range craft for gate-stop logistics and boarding actions. Each shuttle is a lifeboat, a gunship, and the crew's only independent hull; losing one measurably reduced the expedition's options.

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Complement | Pilot + several passengers |
| Drive | Sublight only |
| Defense | Light shields; hull-rated for reentry |
| Role | Landing, salvage, evacuation, ramming if it comes to that |

### 4.2 Drone Network Vessels

**In brief:** The automated enemy: unmanned fighters swarming from carrier command ships, all executing attack logic from a hub world whose builders are long extinct. They engage anything weapons-bearing with perfect coordination and zero self-preservation.

| Class | Size | Role |
|---|---|---|
| Drone fighter | ~10 m **[E]** | Swarm attacker; green energy bolts; expendable by doctrine |
| Command ship | ~500 m **[E]** | Coordinates local fighter groups; shielded; the real target in any engagement |
| Mothership | Several km **[E]** | Strategic carrier and production node |

**Tactical notes:** fighters die easily individually and never individually. Killing the local command ship releases the swarm into confusion, the only kill-chain that works repeatedly. Destruction of the network's central hub (year two) degraded but did not disarm the class; dormant assets remain **[W]**.

**Origin:** their makers died in the war that birthed them; *Destiny* fought them before humanity existed and was still firing on her when the crew arrived. The crew inherited a fifty-million-year-old feud they never joined.

### 4.3 Nakai Vessels

Blue-armored warships of the Nakai species: hyperdrive-capable, shielded, crewed, near-parity with Destiny in a duel and willing to take losses the expedition cannot. Signature capability: abduction raids via short-range transporter-analog beams **[E]**. Withdrawn from the corridor after heavy losses in the drone war; their absence is unexplained and therefore temporary.

---

## PART FIVE: WEAPONS AND EQUIPMENT

### 5.1 Weapons

**In brief:** The expedition fights with what eighty people carried through one gate plus captured Lucian Alliance arms: a handful of P-90s and pistols, a few naquadah-enhanced grenades, Alliance energy rifles after the takeover attempt, and improvised anything.

- **Earth kit:** P-90s, sidearms, limited ammunition, every round is irreplaceable, so fire discipline is religious.
- **Lucian Alliance energy rifles:** post-takeover acquisitions; rechargeable cells make them the de facto standard arm.
- **Destiny's defensive batteries:** ship-scale, minimal, effective against fighters only (§2.5).
- **Improvised ordnance:** the engineering team's specialty, breaching charges, remote detonators, and at least one plan per season that should not have worked.

### 5.2 Kinos

The signature equipment of the volume: floating spherical remotes, camera, microphone, speaker, and light. Uses on record: gate recon, hull inspection, security surveillance, evidence collection (the airlock murder investigation), hostage negotiation, funeral broadcasts, and games. The kino net is the ship's nervous system extension; whoever controls kinos sees everything, which makes kino discipline a standing political issue **[W]**.

### 5.3 Field Equipment

- Communication stones (§3.3), stored under dual control.
- Ancient hand-scanners salvaged from ship stores.
- Water reclamation kits and ration packs: the true currency aboard.
- Duty rosters, consumables ledgers, and the stasis contingency file: paperwork as survival gear.

---

## PART SIX: THREAT DOSSIERS

### 6.0 The Drone Network

**In brief:** An automated war machine outliving its makers: fighters, command ships, motherships (§4.2) executing engagement logic against any armed vessel. They cannot be reasoned with because there is no one home to reason with, the closest thing to a ghost story in this set that is also real.

**Doctrine notes:**

- They escalate: first contact was a single scout; by year two, full fleet engagements.
- They ignore the unarmed, *Destiny* survived her earliest encounters by being too damaged to register as a priority target. That grace period ended.
- Hub destruction scattered them; scattered is not dead. Any post-pin waking should ask what fifty years of autonomous drift taught the machines.

### 6.1 The Nakai

A starfaring species of telepathic blue-skinned humanoids competing for the seeded corridor's resources, water worlds, gates, and (briefly) people. Capabilities on record: abduction raids, mind-probing of captives (Rush), and human augmentation experiments producing hybrid physiology in an abducted crew member (Chloe Armstrong, transformation reversed, scars psychological).

**Assessment:** near-peer adversaries who withdrew rather than face the drone war's winner. Their return is not a question of whether but of which season **[W]**.

### 6.2 The Lucian Alliance Contingent

The takeover faction that gated aboard during the incursion attempt ("Incursion" arc): soldiers and scientists under Kiva's command, absorbed into ship's company after their coup failed and both sides needed every pair of hands.

- **Integration status:** uneasy, permanent. Alliance members serve on duty rosters, sit in the mess, and remember being prisoners of war here.
- **Standing value:** energy weapons, hyperdrive engineering literacy, and the only people aboard who chose none of this and stayed anyway.
- **Risk:** command legitimacy disputes map directly onto the military/civilian/LA triangle; every factional crisis since has tried to recruit them.

**[W]** The LA contingent exists so that no conflict aboard is ever two-sided. Use accordingly.

### 6.3 Internal Fracture (the standing threat)

The honest dossier: the most dangerous force aboard *Destiny* has been *Destiny*'s complement.

- **Legitimacy gap:** Colonel Young commands; Rush holds master codes; civilian leadership (Wray) holds Earth's ear via stones. Three authorities, one air supply.
- **Precedents on record:** a failed mutiny, a marooning (Young left Rush on a planet; the ship came back for both), a murder investigation, election talk.
- **Mitigation:** shared labor, shared danger, and the slow alchemy of duty rosters turning strangers into a crew. It mostly works. "Mostly" is the drama budget **[W]**.

---

## PART SEVEN: COMMAND UNDER STRAIN

### 7.0 Chain of Command

```
Homeworld Command (Earth)      — stones-only liaison; no operational reach
        |
COLONEL EVERETT YOUNG          — expedition commander
        |
   +--------+------------------+
   |        |                   |
MILITARY  CIVILIAN COUNCIL    TECHNICAL AUTHORITY
(Marines,  (Wray et al.:       (Rush + engineering:
 security)  governance, morale) master codes, the Bridge)
   |
LUCIAN ALLIANCE DETACHMENT     — absorbed; loyalty conditional
```

**In brief:** This is the only volume in the set where the org chart is itself a contested document. Young's command rests on necessity and consent, not relief schedules; Rush's technical authority rests on being irreplaceable; the council's moral authority rests on Earth's mandate that nobody believes in when the air scrubbers fail.

**Doctrinal rules that emerged:**

1. Medical authority (TJ) outranks convenience.
2. Stones-to-Earth traffic runs through the commander, no private channels.
3. The Bridge requires dual presence: command plus technical. Neither goes alone. (Rule honored mostly in the breach; see §6.3.)
4. Stasis selection criteria were drafted, argued over, and signed before anyone entered a pod, the fairest thing this crew ever did was pre-commit to fairness while they still could.

---

## PART EIGHT: A VOID BESTIARY

*Phenomena of the intergalactic dark.*

### 8.0 The Signal

**What:** A pattern embedded in the cosmic microwave background, structure older than the universe it sits inside, pointing somewhere. *Destiny*'s entire mission is to follow it; her instruments read it continuously.
**Where:** Everywhere. It is in the floor of reality.
**Cost of contact:** Meaning. Everyone who studies it long enough starts believing something about it. The manual takes no position on what the Signal *is*, that is the last story this franchise gets to tell **[W]**.

### 8.1 Eden

**What:** A world encountered once, off-course and impossibly idyllic, temperate, fruiting, empty. Verified real by soil samples; unverified in origin. The leading theory: a gift or test by beings capable of building paradise, offered to a crew being ground down by their ship.
**Where:** Not where it was. The ship moved on; one crew member stayed behind by choice.
**Cost of contact:** Hope with no supply line. Those who saw Eden argue about it still.

### 8.2 Novus and the Descendants

**What:** An advanced human colony founded by *Destiny*'s own crew, from an alternate timeline in which the ship failed and they settled, married, built, and died for two thousand years before their descendants re-encountered the ship in the present.
**Where:** A gated world two thousand years downstream of a history that no longer happened.
**Cost of contact:** Grief with grandchildren attached. The colonists keep museums of their founders; the founders are alive, younger than their own statues.

### 8.3 Star-Diving

**What:** The routine hazard of charging: shielded corona passes through living stars. Shield decay mid-dive has caused casualties; dive windows are computed, not negotiated.
**Where:** Any stellar type on route; the hotter the star, the faster the charge.
**Cost of contact:** Measured in seconds of margin. Every dive is controlled catastrophe practice.

### 8.4 Franklin's Door

**What:** The interface chair's deepest session left one operator vanished, physically absent, plausibly merged with the ship's systems. The Bridge sometimes behaves as if someone is home.
**Where:** Wherever he is. The chair room smells of ozone when asked.
**Cost of contact:** Undefined by design. Treat all "the ship did it herself" events as potentially his handwriting **[W]**.

### 8.5 Alternate-Timeline Bleed

**What:** Twin-*Destiny* class events: wormhole anomalies near stars duplicating ships and crews, alternate histories leaving physical debris (and graves) in the primary timeline.
**Where:** Stellar proximity incidents; see also companion manuals' quantum mirror doctrine (SG-1 §8.3).
**Cost of contact:** Proof that other yous made other calls, and occasionally, that their consequences arrive in your mail.

### 8.6 Stasis Technology

**What:** Ancient pods suspending metabolism indefinitely; *Destiny* carries a full complement rated for multi-year crossings.
**Where:** Dedicated stasis deck; pods networked to automated recovery protocols.
**Cost of contact:** Time debt, everyone who sleeps pays in a universe that moved on without them. The pin-date crew are its current account holders.

---

## APPENDICES

### Appendix A: Glossary

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Black gate | Destiny's pre-network stargate; ship-dialed, no DHD |
| Command ship | Drone-network carrier coordinating fighter swarms |
| Communication stones | Consciousness-swap devices linking Destiny and Earth |
| Drone fighters | Unmanned swarm craft of the extinct builders' war |
| FTL | Destiny's direct-translation drive; faster than hyperdrive, untrackable mid-flight |
| Hub | Central drone command world (destroyed) |
| Icarus Project | Naquadria-core nine-chevron dialing program that found Destiny |
| Kino | Floating spherical remote camera/monitor |
| Master codes | Rush's private override authority over ship systems |
| Nine-chevron address | Dial requiring planetary-scale power; reaches unique deep-space targets |
| Novus | Descendant colony from an alternate timeline |
| Seed ship | Automated gate-laying predecessor vessel |
| Signal, the | CMB pattern defining Destiny's mission |
| Star-scoop / diving | Shielded stellar corona charging maneuver |
| Stones | See communication stones |
| Stasis pods | Long-duration suspension system (executed at pin date) |

### Appendix B: Units and Travel Times

| Route | Method | Duration |
|---|---|---|
| Gate stop → next gate stop | Ship's schedule | Days–weeks between stellar encounters **[E]** |
| Interstellar crossing | FTL segment | Hours–days **[E]** |
| Intergalactic void crossing | Sustained FTL | Years — the pin-date jump is one |
| Destiny ↔ Earth (matter) | None available | N/A — stones only |
| Destiny ↔ Earth (minds) | Communication stones | Instant |

### Appendix C: Canon Discrepancy Log and Writer's Latitude

| Topic | On-screen variance | Manual ruling |
|---|---|---|
| Destiny length | Never stated | ~800 m **[E]** pin, consistent with shuttle/gate proportions |
| Shuttle count | Two seen, three implied | Ruled two surviving, one lost **[E]** (§4.1) |
| FTL vs. hyperspace speed | "Faster," never quantified | Qualitative ruling stands (§2.6); no numbers on screen |
| Drone hub destruction | Fleet degraded, not eliminated | Residual threat persists post-pin (§6.0) |
| Franklin's fate | Deliberately unresolved | Remains unresolved by ruling (§8.4) |
| The Signal's meaning | Never revealed | Never revealed by ruling — it is the franchise's sealed room (§8.0) |
| Nakai return | Left open | Open by ruling; withdrawal ≠ exit (§6.1) |
| Complement size | ~80 implied | Pinned ~80 **[E]** |
| Stone-swap ethics | Inconsistent oversight | §3.3 rules stand: dual control, commander's channel |
| Post-pin stories | None filmed | All are *wakings* — something changed while they slept (§1.5) |

**Writer's latitude principles:** identical to companion volumes, every miracle leaves a bill somewhere. Aboard Destiny the bills are paid in air, water, ammunition, trust, and time; the ledger is the story.

<!-- ====== VOLUME: BABYLON 5 ====== -->

# VOLUME - BABYLON 5

*Known Galaxy - pin 2260 Shadow War*

---

# BABYLON 5: TECHNICAL MANUAL

**BABYLON STATION OPERATIONS REFERENCE, FIRST EDITION**

*Fourth entry in the collected set, companion to the Stargate program volumes. Prepared in the tradition of the Star Trek Writers'/DS9 Technical Manuals: an in-universe reference consolidating broadcast canon, with clearly marked engineering extrapolation.*

---

## ABOUT THIS DOCUMENT

This manual provides the detailed technical background that stories aboard Babylon 5 and across the known galaxy sometimes need. It supplements character work, never replaces it, Babylon 5's stories are about choices made under pressure of history, and every technical system here exists to raise the price of those choices.

**Edition pin.** This First Edition reflects the station's status quo as of **2260 CE, the Shadow War open, Babylon 5 in open secession from the Earth Alliance** (see §1.5). Events after that point are not reflected here.

**Conventions used throughout:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Established by on-screen canon as broadcast (the TV movies count as broadcast) |
| **[E]** | Extrapolation — internally consistent engineering detail where the screen was silent |
| **[W]** | Writer's latitude note — guidance on how hard this rule binds you |

Where this manual conflicts with a filmed episode, the episode wins. Log the conflict in Appendix C.

**Structure:** Part One sets the galactic theater. Part Two is the station herself. Part Three covers jumpgate and hyperspace operations. Part Four carries vessel specifications. Part Five covers weapons and equipment. Part Six is the threat dossier, read it before writing any Shadow, Vorlon, or Psi Corps scene. Part Seven defines command structure. Part Eight catalogs phenomena. Appendices carry glossary, conversions, the canon log, and index.

---

## PART ONE: THE GALACTIC THEATER

### 1.0 Introduction

Babylon 5 is the last of the Babylon Project: five kilometers of diplomatic neutrality parked in orbit around a dead world at the galaxy's busiest jumpgate junction, built so that a war ten centuries old could end at a table instead of in orbit. Her crew are humans running a hotel for empires, every one of whom has fought everyone else within living memory.

**In brief:** Where other installations defend territory, Babylon 5 manufactures something rarer: conversations between powers that have no other common ground.

### 1.1 The Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Known Galaxy — Earth Alliance space, the Rim, and the League of Non-Aligned Worlds |
| Installation | Babylon 5 Station, Epsilon III orbit |
| Complement | ~250,000 residents and transients **[E]**; staff complement ~2,500 **[E]** |
| Posture | Independent state as of the edition pin — seceded from the Earth Alliance under President Clark's dictatorship |
| Neutrality | Charter-bound open port: four governments plus the League maintain permanent delegations |

Three facts define every decision aboard:

1. **Neutrality is the product.** The station survives because everyone needs a place to talk. Every arrest, every favor, every intercepted shipment spends from that account.
2. **Two ancient wars run underneath everything.** The Shadows and the Vorlons have recruited younger races as proxies for a million years; the current war is their argument made kinetic.
3. **Earth cannot help.** The homeworld is under a totalitarian presidency; the station's garrison is loyal to the charter, not the flag, a fact with a body count.

**[W]** B5 stories are history stories: individuals bent against structures larger than they are. Technology should always serve that pressure, never solve it cheaply.

### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Era | Event |
|---|---|
| ~1,000,000 years ago | First Ones dominate; Vorlons and Shadows become rival shepherds of younger races — order versus chaos as teaching doctrine |
| ~10,000 years ago | Most First Ones depart beyond the Rim; the two shepherds remain |
| ~1,260 years ago | Last great Shadow War. Valen leads the united Minbari to victory; the Grey Council founds on that victory; Shadows sleep |
| 2229–2232 | Dilgar War: the Dilgar rampage through the League until the Earth Alliance enters and breaks them |
| 2245–2248 | Earth–Minbari War: first contact catastrophe at the hands of an Earth ship; Minbari genocide campaign halted at the Line when they discover Valen's soul among humans. Earth broken, humbled, paranoid |
| 2248–2257 | Babylon Stations 1–4: sabotage, bankruptcy, disappearance (Babylon 4 — see §8.2). Babylon 5 completes under private Earth financing |
| 2258 | Station's first year: Kosh's arrival, assassination politics, Raider suppression, the Soul Hunter incident |
| 2259 | Death of President Santiago; Vice-President Clark assumes power. Shadow vessel recovered at Zagros; first open Shadow move in a thousand years |
| 2260 | Shadow War erupts openly. Babylon 5 secedes rather than submit to Clark. **Edition pin** |
### 1.3 Technological Assessment of Major Powers

*(Full dossiers in Part Six. Format follows the set's assessment convention.)*

**Earth Alliance.** The youngest major power, barely a century of jumpspace travel, compensating with mass production and stubbornness. Omega-class destroyers rotate for gravity; Starfury wings punch above their weight; Earthforce doctrine is artillery-first and unimaginative. Politically the Alliance is the theater's sick man: a presidency sliding into fascism while its best fleet sits at Babylon 5.

**Minbari Federation.** Technically superior to everyone (gravitic drives, stealth hulls, beam cannons measured in seconds-to-kill), constrained by caste politics and religious law from using any of it aggressively since Valen's war. Their fleet is the galaxy's trump card, held by a warrior caste spoiling for redemption.

**Centauri Republic.** Declining imperial power running on prestige, slave-adjacent client states, and superb light ships. Their renaissance under Cartagia's court is the Shadow alliance made flesh, nostalgia weaponized.

**Narn Regime.** Occupied for a century by the Centauri, liberated by guerrilla attrition; now rebuilding on salvaged Centauri tech and bitter clarity. Their heavy cruisers are honest, slow, and expendable, like their strategy after the fall of their homeworld.

**The League of Non-Aligned Worlds.** A dozen minor powers (Drazi, Brakiri, Abbai, Pak'ma'ra, Vree and kin) whose combined fleets matter and whose separate fleets do not. Babylon 5 exists to make them add up.

**The Shadows.** See §6.0. The oldest power in play: ships that think, allies that vanish, and a philosophy that breeds strength through engineered catastrophe.

**The Vorlons.** See §6.1. Order as an ideology, inscrutable, patient, and generous in ways that always invoice later.

### 1.4 Founding Technologies of Note

**In brief:** Three technologies shape every strategic equation in this volume:

1. **Jumpgates and hyperspace beacons**, the only FTL transit system (§3.0); controlling gates means controlling trade, war, and escape.
2. **Artificial gravity asymmetry**, Minbari/Vorlon gravitic mastery versus Earth's rotating-section workaround defines ship design philosophy galaxy-wide.
3. **Telepathy**, a human minority (one in a thousand) with commercial, military, and political value enough to justify an institution (the Psi Corps) that functions as a state within the state.

### 1.5 Status Quo Snapshot (Edition Pin: 2260 CE)

| Domain | Status |
|---|---|
| Station | Independent; secession defended by Starfury wing + White Star support **[W]** |
| Earth relations | Open hostility — Clark's regime lists B5 command as traitors; Nightwatch infiltrators purged or hiding aboard |
| Shadow War | Open. Colony worlds vanishing; League members requesting B5's coalition leadership |
| Minbari | Religious caste backs the station's war effort via covert White Star operations; warrior caste obstructs — Federation near civil split |
| Centauri | Shadow-aligned at court level; Republic forces hitting Narn and League targets |
| Vorlons | One ambassador resident (Kosh); minimal, cryptic, decisive when it moves |
| Psi Corps | Officially Earth institution; unofficially playing every side |

**[W]** This snapshot is the war-room page. Any story may move one or two lines; the Shadow War exists so that no line stays still for long.
---

## PART TWO: BABYLON 5 STATION

### 2.0 Station Overview

**In brief:** The last of the Babylon Project's diplomatic stations: a five-mile O'Neill-cylinder habitat spun for gravity, strung along a spine of docking bays, wrapped in the known galaxy's densest diplomatic charter. Built by Earth, financed by Minbari gold, staffed by everyone.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Class | Babylon-class diplomatic station (final unit of five) |
| Length | 8,064.5 m (stated on-screen in "And Now for a Word") |
| Displacement | ~2.5 million metric tons **[E]** |
| Gravity | Rotational — ~0.9 g in habitation ring **[E]**; zero-g spine and docking bays |
| Complement | ~2,500 staff **[E]**; 250,000 residents/transients capacity **[E]** |
| Autonomy | Self-sufficient for years: agriculture ring, fusion plant, ore processing |
| Defense | 48 SA-23E Starfuries (two squadrons) + interceptor grid + light pulse cannon array **[E]** |

**Design language:** functional Earthforce industrial, grey-green cylinder, blue-lit command sphere at the forward end, red-and-white docking spines. She is not beautiful; she is *working*.

```
   BABYLON 5 — PROFILE (schematic, not to scale)

     C&C SPHERE          HABITATION RING            DOCKING SPINES
      .-"""-.              ____________________
     /  C&C  \____________/                    \_______
    |  (blue) |  Blue Sector ->  Green Sector  |       |
    |_________|  (command/medlab) (diplomats)  | BAYS  |
        ||         Grey Sector (industry)      | 1-6?  |
        ||         Sector code (cargo/slums)   |_______|
     ___||_____ CAROUSEL AGRICULTURE RING ___________
    / FUSION CORE, FUEL, ENGINEERING (zero-g spine) \
   `-------------------------------------------------'
```

### 2.01 Zone Deep-Dive

**Blue Sector (command spine).**

| Facility | Function |
|---|---|
| Command & Control | Traffic, defense, jumpgate ops (§2.2) |
| Medlab 1–3 | Primary care, quarantine isolation, xenobiology labs |
| Station jail / holding | Charter-processing detention |
| Officer quarters & war-room annexes | Briefing theater adjacent to C&C |
| Ambassadorial airlocks | Delegation docking with diplomatic privilege screening |

**Green Sector (embassy ring).** Each major power holds a self-sealing wing with its own atmosphere mix on request, Vorlon quarters cannot be entered by any younger race's key. Green Sector is extraterritorial ground by charter; security incidents there are treaty events, not crimes.

**Grey Sector & Downbelow.** Industry, maintenance shops, recycling plants, and the undocumented population living in maintenance crawls. Station security patrols the corridors; nobody patrols Downbelow except its own.

**The Zócalo.** Open market on the habitation ring: commerce, smuggling, news, and the closest thing the station has to a public square. Every plot that needs "somewhere neutral aboard a neutral station" happens here.

**Carousel agriculture ring.** Hydroponic parks doubling as food production and the station's only open green space, weddings, duels, and secret meetings all book the same gardens.

### 2.1 Structures and Zones

| Zone | Function | Access |
|---|---|---|
| Blue Sector | Command & Control, medlabs, officer quarters, jail | Staff only |
| Green Sector | Diplomatic quarters — one wing per embassy | Delegation-controlled |
| Grey Sector | Industry, maintenance, Downbelow's slums | Mixed — the station's conscience lives here |
| Cargo/Sector codes | Warehousing, processing | Commercial |
| Habitation ring (Carousel) | Agriculture parks, housing, Zócalo market | Public |
| Docking spines & bays | Customs, freight, fighter launch rails | Controlled |

**Downbelow** is not on any official chart: the low-ceilinged crawl between Grey and cargo sectors where the undocumented live. Every station story eventually goes there; it is where the charter's promises meet rent.

### 2.2 Command Systems

**Command & Control (C&C).** The blue sphere at the bow: traffic control, defense coordination, jumpgate operation, and the interceptor grid. Watchstanders manage thousands of daily movements; C&C is the single point where the station's neutrality is enforced minute-to-minute.

**Station operations doctrine:** the commanding officer holds civil authority inside the charter and military authority over the garrison, a deliberately doubled mandate that has forced every commander to choose which hat dies first when Earth and the charter conflict.

### 2.3 Computer Systems

Earthforce-standard networked mainframes with voice interface layers; no sapient AI aboard **[W]**, resist the urge; the show's intelligence threats are institutional, not silicon.

- Customs and security databases track every arrival's declared purpose; Downbelow exists in the gap between database and reality.
- The station's charter text outranks station regulations in law, a hierarchy tested on-screen repeatedly.
- Encryption: Earthforce standard plus delegation-specific ciphers; intercepts are C&C's quiet specialty.
### 2.4 Power Generation

**In brief:** Eight fusion reactors **[E]** along the zero-g spine feed the rotation drive, life support, defense grid, and the jumpgate's capacitors.

| Source | Notes |
|---|---|
| Fusion plant cluster | Primary; fuel from the agriculture ring's waste cycle plus ore-processing byproducts |
| Jumpgate capacitor bank | The gate draws station-scale surges per activation — scheduled against defense-grid readiness |
| Solar/auxiliary | Emergency margin only |

**Power discipline:** the interceptor grid and the jumpgate compete for peak draw. A gate activation during an attack is a command decision, not a switch, canon demonstrated when the grid had to choose between fighters and escape routes.

### 2.5 Defensive Systems

**The interceptor grid.** Webbed emitter panels across the hull that fire pulsed energy to detonate incoming ordnance at standoff range, Earthforce's signature answer to superior alien firepower. Effectiveness scales with charge; sustained bombardment strips it section by section.

**Pulse cannon array.** Station-scale pulse batteries for close defense; adequate against raiders, symbolic against capital ships.

**Starfury wing.** Forty-eight SA-23E interceptors (§4.1) launched from spine rails, the station's real sword. Launch tempo under combat: seconds per fighter down the rail **[E]**.

**Structural doctrine:** the station cannot outrun or outfight a battle fleet. Her defenses buy hours for diplomacy, evacuation, or the cavalry, never victory in open battle. Every siege story should honor that math.

### 2.6 Docking and Bay Operations

Six major bays handle freighters, liners, and delegation cutters; fighter rails run parallel. Customs is the station's first line of both revenue and security, every crate logged, every hold scanned, every lie filed for later leverage.

### 2.7 Sensors and Communications

- **Long-range:** hyperspace beacon monitoring doubles as strategic sensor watch, ship movements between gates are visible to anyone reading the beacons (§3.0).
- **Tachyon comm relays:** real-time transmission via tachyon channels within linked space; B5 relays diplomatic traffic for the League.
- **Minbari sensors outclass everything here** — a standing intelligence asymmetry in the Federation's favor.

### 2.8 Life Support and Environment

Closed-cycle atmosphere with sector-level scrubbing; agriculture ring supplies food and CO₂ processing. Downbelow's atmosphere is technically the same air and socially another world. Quarantine medicine is excellent, Medlab 1 handled the Drafa plague (§8.5), but bed capacity is finite; plague stories must respect triage.

### 2.9 Emergency Operations

| Condition | Trigger | Response |
|---|---|---|
| Intrusion | Boarding action, sabotage | Security lockdown by zone; fighter alert |
| Bomb threat | Charter violation | Customs trace + Grey Sector sweep |
| Siege | Capital threat | Grid full-power, civilians to shelter, gate reserved for evacuation |
| Secession | Government illegitimate | Charter invocation — executed at pin date; garrison loyalty decided it |

---

## PART THREE: JUMPGATE AND HYPERSPACE OPERATIONS

### 3.0 The Jump Network

**In brief:** FTL travel in the known galaxy runs exclusively through hyperspace, a layered dimension entered via fixed jumpgates (vortex generators of Elder-race vintage) or ship-mounted jump engines. Inside, there are no stars: navigation is by beacon chain, and leaving the beacons is how crews vanish.

**Rules of the road:**

- **Gates are furniture of empires.** Who builds a gate owns who passes through it; the Shadows' war strategy begins with destroying gates and beacons.
- **Beacon discipline:** ships follow signal buoys like mountain ropes in fog. Deviate meters off-line and you can emerge years away or never.
- **Jump vortices kill.** Anything organic in a forming vortex is shredded; formations approach gates in sequence, not mass.
- **Combat in hyperspace** is blind brawling in fog, sensors read beacons only, weapons fire risks breaching into realspace at random. Doctrine: fight in realspace, hide in hyperspace.

**[W]** Hyperspace is this volume's ocean: storms, sea monsters (§8.4), and drownings included. Its rules exist so that being lost means something.
---

## PART FOUR: VESSELS

**Recognition reference:** see plate `Babylon 5 - Ship Recognition Plate.png` (companion file, this folder).

### 4.1 Earth Alliance Vessels

#### SA-23E Starfury

**In brief:** The galaxy's best pure-space fighter flown by its youngest power: a cross-axled cannon with engines at four corners, able to pivot on any axis without turning. No atmospheric capability, a Starfury in air is a brick with guns.

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Crew | 1 |
| Drive | 4-corner fusion thrusters; true 6DOF Newtonian maneuvering |
| Weapons | 4 pulse cannons (wing-root pairs); up to 8 hardpoints **[E]** |
| Defense | Light armor; ejection seat |
| Role | Interception, patrol, carrier ops from rails |

**Doctrine:** Starfuries fight like knights, not planes, turn fights are three-dimensional chess. The design's honesty (no gravity, no atmosphere, no illusions) makes it the EA's most beloved machine.

#### SA-32A Thunderbolt

Two-seat strike variant: heavier cannons, missile racks, cockpit for a weapons officer.

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Length × width × height | 15.54 m × 18.7 m (foils extended) × 6.96 m — production-set verified |
| Weapons | 4 heavy pulse cannons + missile hardpoints |
| Role | Strike, escort, fleet defense |

Slower, harder-hitting; introduced as Earth's fleet modernized under Clark.

#### Hyperion-Class Heavy Cruiser **[E]**

The Omega's predecessor: pre-war cruiser lineage still flying patrol duties across the Rim. Lighter armor, older pulse/laser mix, no fighter-recovery hangar worth the name, Hyperions fight as gunlines and die as gunlines when outnumbered. Retained in service because the Alliance builds destroyers slower than it loses cruisers.

### 4.1a Omega-Class Destroyer (full sheet)

| Specification | Value (production-verified figures) |
|---|---|
| Length | 1,714.3 m |
| Mass | 44.6 million metric tons |
| Crew / troops | ~850–1,000 crew; up to 18,000 troop capacity |
| Fighters | 36 (Starfury/Thunderbolt wings) |
| Power | 4 fusion reactors |
| Endurance | ~18 months autonomous |
| Armament | 6 heavy particle/laser cannons · 6 heavy pulse cannons · 12 medium particle lasers · 12 medium pulse cannons · 2 fusion-missile launchers |
| Defense | 8–10 m armored hull; 18 Mk II interceptor-grid projectors |
| Design flaw | Rotating habitat must halt spin for jump/engagement integration (§4.1 doctrine) |

**Systems-by-station:**

| Station | Function |
|---|---|
| Bridge (rotating section top) | Command when spun; hands off to battle bridge during combat transitions |
| Hangar bay | 36-fighter recovery through stern doors under rotation lockout |
| Troop decks | 18,000 GROPOS billets — the Omega is also Earth's army transport |
| Engineering spine | 4-reactor grid with load-shedding between weapons and rotation drive |

**Doctrine:** Omegas win wars of position and lose duels of initiative. Their jump ritual (§4.1) remains the exploitable moment every enemy trains against. Advanced Omegas, Clark-era refits with Shadow-derived bio-armor and upgraded fusion cannons, exist as black-program exceptions **[W]**; encountering one is a campaign arc, not an encounter.

#### Omega-Class Destroyer

**In brief:** Earthforce's capital workhorse, a kilometer of guns and armor wrapped around a rotating habitat section, because Earth cannot build artificial gravity.

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Length | ~1,700 m **[E]** |
| Complement | ~1,100 **[E]** |
| Drive | Beam fusion drives; spin section must stop rotation to jump or fight **[W]** — the Omega's fatal ritual |
| Weapons | Pulse cannon batteries, plasma cannons, missile racks; no beam weapons (pre-Warlock) |
| Defense | Ablative armor; interceptor grid |

**Doctrine:** Omegas fight in gunlines. Their jump ritual, stop the spin, open the vortex, commit, is the single most exploitable moment in any engagement against them.

#### Warlock-Class (preview generation) **[E]**

The post-Omega generation: artificial gravity experiments, beam main armament, Shadow-war lessons absorbed into hull form. Few exist by pin date; treat sightings as plot events.

### 4.2 Minbari Vessels

#### White Star Class

**In brief:** The war's decisive weapon: a Vorlon-Minbari hybrid gunship small enough to be fast, alive enough to heal, and armed with a main beam that flirts with Vorlon technology.

| Specification | Value (production-verified figures) |
|---|---|
| Length | 475.6 m |
| Crew | ~50 |
| Drive | Stalasha gravimetric engines — *gate-free jump capability* (creates own vortex) **[W]** |
| Gravity | Internal artificial gravity (Vorlon systems) |
| Weapons | 1 quantum discharge beam cannon (primary) · 2 neutron cannons · 2 twin fusion cannon turrets |
| Defense | Vorlon adaptive defense system; magnetic discharge beams; organic self-repair hull |
| Auxiliary | 4 Nial heavy fighters docked |

**Doctrine:** White Stars win by refusing the terms of engagement, they dodge what should hit and heal what doesn't. Fleet strength reached 100–150 hulls by the War's height, built at Valen's Eye yards. The class is the coalition's only answer to battle crabs, and everyone knows it. Destroyer-scale White Stars enter production post-pin via an Earth-Minbari joint venture **[E]**.

#### Sharlin-Class Warcruiser

The Federation's capital expression: 1,600 m length / 1,900 m height (accepted scale), gravitic drive, electromagnetic stealth that made Earth targeting useless for the whole war, molecular beam cannons capable of carving cruisers in salvos. One Sharlin was worth an Earth task force; the warrior caste's refusal to forget that fact shapes every council session.

**Sharlin systems-by-station:**

| Station | Function |
|---|---|
| Command circle | Triluminary-keyed command; caste leadership embarked in wartime |
| Beam batteries | Molecular cannons with sustained-cut mode — hull-carving rather than shell-fire doctrine |
| Stealth array | Electromagnetic absorption — targeting solutions require visual-range or Minbari consent **[W]** |
| Fighter bays | Nial heavy fighter wings |

#### Tinashi War Frigate **[E]**

Cruiser-weight escort (~900 m **[E]**): same stealth/beam family at patrol scale; the Federation's everyday presence on the Rim.

### 4.3 Centauri & Narn Vessels

#### Primus Battlecruiser

The Republic's heavy line (~1,300 m **[E]**): eight forward battle lasers in the classic "battle crab claw" arrangement, ion torpedoes, gravitic drive. Elegant, aging, and sufficient against everyone the Republic still bothers to fight.

#### Vorchan Warcruiser **[E]**

Fast attack cruiser (~500 m **[E]**): light battle laser, high acceleration, the Centauri raider's choice for the Narn campaign's dirty work.

#### G'Quan-Class Heavy Cruiser

The Narn backbone (~1,400 m **[E]**): energy mines (area-denial plasma bursts, unique in known space), heavy pulse arrays, bought-and-copied Centauri lineage. Slow, honest, expendable, like the regime that flies them after the fall.

#### Toreth-Class Gunboat **[E]**

System-defense cutter (~200 m **[E]**): pulse cannons, minimal endurance; the Narn militia's last ships.
### 4.4 Elder-Race Vessels

#### Shadow Battle Crab

**In brief:** A living warship, black multi-jointed spider of semi-organic armor that thinks, heals, and screams on sensors when destroyed. Its main beam slices any younger-race capital in one pass; its fighters are piloted by beings merged into the hull.

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Length | ~2,000 m variable (organic) **[E]** |
| Complement | Merged crew + sentient core |
| Weapons | Slicing beam (capital one-shot); fighter launch |
| Defense | Bio-armor that absorbs and adapts; only Vorlon-scale power or the fleet-killing tricks of the last War scratch it |

**Doctrine:** battle crabs do not fight fleets; they teach them. Appearances are calibrated to provoke, test, or punish. When they stop testing, the real war has started.

#### Vorlon Transport & Cruiser

Organic saucers of impossible capability: self-repair, energy absorption that eats incoming fire, and weapons scaled to whatever insulted them. Vorlons escalate exactly as much as required and never explain. Contact protocols: courtesy, patience, and no sudden philosophies.

---

## PART FIVE: WEAPONS AND EQUIPMENT

### 5.1 Personal Weapons

**In brief:** The galaxy's infantry fights with pulse plasma, Earth's PPG family is the standard by which everyone else's sidearm is measured.

- **PPG (Phased Plasma Gun) pistol:** standard sidearm; cauterizing burns, low recoil, ammo cells.
- **PPG rifle:** squad support variant; longer cell life, underbarrel options **[E]**.
- **Centauri/Narn equivalents:** energy pistols of comparable effect; Narn models favor ruggedness over elegance.
- **Minbari fighting pike:** collapsible staff weapon, a reminder that the galaxy's best soldiers still train to close distance.
- **Projectile weapons:** rare but decisive against targets immune to energy bleed; Earth marines keep slug throwers for exactly those guests.

### 5.2 Ship and Station Weapons

| Class | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Interceptor grid | Point-defense energy web | EA signature; strips incoming fire at range |
| Pulse cannons | Bolt-projectors | EA/League standard; rate-of-fire doctrine |
| Plasma streams / fusion cannons | Minbari medium guns | Secondaries on Sharlin/White Star |
| Molecular beam cannons | Sustained cutting beams | Minbari primaries; carve hulls in salvos |
| Battle lasers | Centauri heavy beams | Primus forward battery; G'Quan copies |
| Energy mines | Narn area-denial bursts | Unique tactical niche; formation-breakers |
| Slicing beam | Shadow primary | One-pass capital kill (§6.0) |
| Gravitic/neutron beams | Elder-race primaries | Vorlon scale; do not duel these |

### 5.3 Strategic Materials and Systems

- **Hull alloys:** EA morbidium steel **[E]** vs. Minbari crystalline-molecular composites vs. Shadow organic armor, three design philosophies: mass, precision, and adaptation.
- **Gravitic systems:** Minbari/Vorlon mastery versus rotating sections, the galaxy's starkest technology gap.
- **Telepathy infrastructure:** Psi Corps training, drugs (sleepers), and implant tech, telepaths are strategic materiel in every sense (§6.2).

---

## PART SIX: THREAT DOSSIERS

### 6.0 The Shadows

**In brief:** The oldest of the two surviving First Ones: a species ten million years old whose entire civilization runs on one theorem, *younger races evolve fastest through chaos, war, and fear.* They seed themselves among younger worlds as advisors and agents, engineer conflicts as curricula, and sleep for millennia between lessons.

**More:**

- **Philosophy as weapon:** the Shadows rarely conquer; they *teach*. Their question to every young race is "what do you want?", asked in ways that burn continents.
- **Ships:** living battle crabs (§4.4) that merge pilots into hulls; their death-scream on telepathic frequencies is a battlefield hazard all its own.
- **Servants:** corrupted humans (Morden's line), Drakh retainers **[E]**, and kept creatures, the Shadows recruit ambition, not ideology.
- **Weakness:** their method requires their own restraint, they fight to teach, not exterminate. Telepathic disruption disorients their merged crews (the last War's lesson). And their philosophy can be *answered* rather than defeated: ask the counter-question.

**Engagement rules:** Never negotiate from fear; it reads as an answer. Never trust an advisor who arrives after a disaster offering help. Always ask what lesson the attack was designed to teach.

### 6.1 The Vorlons

**In brief:** The other shepherd: order incarnate, inscrutable by policy, generous by invoice. Vorlon contact suits hide their true forms, each viewer sees something holy, because unshielded Vorlon presence rewrites younger minds.

- **Method:** long cultivation of chosen races, cryptic aid ("understanding is a three-edged sword"), and escalation so measured it reads as prophecy.
- **Threat profile:** none declared, all implied. A Vorlon cruiser absorbing a League fleet's full fire and asking "are you finished?" is diplomacy.
- **The invoice:** every Vorlon gift advances their order-versus-chaos argument. By the edition pin, both shepherds have stopped teaching and started proving, which makes them the war, not the referees.

**[W]** Write Vorlons as weather with opinions. The moment they explain themselves plainly, they shrink.
### 6.2 The Psi Corps

**In brief:** Earth's telepath institution, orphanage, school, employer, and surveillance state for the one-in-a-thousand humans born with telepathy. Officially a professional guild; functionally a state within the state that answers to itself.

- **The rating system:** P1–P12 scales ability; P12s can read and rewrite minds. Deep-scan evidence is legally admissible in Alliance courts, which makes every commercial transaction a potential mind-crime scene.
- **Institutional structure:** a Directorate of Metasensory Affairs (Earth liaison), internal Psi Cops (enforcement), and the breeding program pairing high-rated telepaths by genetic mandate, consent optional **[W]**.
- **Sleeper processing:** personality-overwrite implants ("sleepers") turn captured rogues into return-to-source assets; discovery of an implant is a station-wide security event.
- **Rogue problem:** unregistered telepaths fleeing mandatory Corps service form the station's permanent underground, Downbelow's most valuable residents and its most dangerous.
- **War relevance:** telepaths are among the few proven weapons against Shadow merged crews (§6.0), which converts an Earth civil-rights question into a strategic asset overnight.

**[W]** The Corps exists to make civil-liberties scenes carry body counts. Use them for stories where the safe choice and the right choice have different casualty lists.

#### Corps Org Chart

```
PSI CORPS DIRECTORATE
        |
  +-----+--------+-------------+
  |                |           |
METACO POLICE    CADRE       COMMERCIAL
("Psi Cops")   (training     HOUSES (licensing
(P12 manhunts)  academies)    telepaths to industry)
```

### 6.3 Nightwatch and the Homegrown Threat

Earth's Ministry of Peace volunteer auxiliary: patriotic citizens deputized into political enforcement. On Babylon 5 they operated as a loyalty cell until the secession purge. Their dossier is short and necessary: fascism arrives as paperwork, wears your uniform, and quotes your own charter back at you.

### 6.4 Raiders

Pirate bands preying on the Rim's thin shipping using salvaged warships and gate ambush tactics. Professional hazard, not strategic threat, except when someone pays them to be one. Raider activity spiking around a quiet world is intelligence, not crime statistics.

### 6.5 The Drakh **[E]**

Shadow retainers and heirs: a servitor race whose fleets and cruelty outlive their patrons' withdrawal. Post-pin antagonists by design; sightings during the War were deniable and deniable things are this manual's favorite kind.

---

## PART SEVEN: COMMAND AND ORGANIZATION

### 7.0 Chain of Command

```
EARTH ALLIANCE SENATE (pre-secession)   — charter authority
        |
Babylon Project oversight / Advisory Council
        |
COMMANDER, BABYLON 5 STATION            — civil + military authority
        |
   +--------+-------------+---------------+
   |        |             |               |
SECURITY  OPERATIONS   MEDICAL         DIPLOMATIC STAFF
(station   (C&C,        (Medlab 1-3,     (one delegation
 police,    fighters,    quarantine)       per power)
 customs)   docking)
   |
NIGHTWATCH (purged at pin date)  — loyalty cell, dissolved by force
```

**In brief:** The commander's authority is doubled (civil charter + military garrison) and therefore permanently conflicted; the Advisory Council gives four embassies plus League voices formal say in station governance, a parliament disguised as a staff meeting.

- **Medical override:** Medlab holds quarantine authority over all traffic, including delegations.
- **Customs as foreign policy:** every inspection is a message to somebody's government.
- **Post-secession reality:** Earth orders are void aboard; the council recognizes the station as sovereign by necessity. Every diplomatic consequence of that recognition is story fuel.

### 7.1 Personnel Policies

- **Telepath registration:** Earth law requires Corps membership or suppression drugs; B5 honors rogue sanctuary informally, a standing treaty violation with Earth, maintained deliberately.
- **Garrison composition:** Earthforce Marines and fighter wing; post-pin, augmented by Minbari-religious-caste volunteers and League detachments **[W]**.
- **Rules of engagement:** charter-first, weapons only after identification and warning; delegations are inviolate short of overt attack.

---

## PART EIGHT: A GALAXY BESTIARY

*Phenomena, artifacts, and hazards.*

### 8.0 The Great Machine (Epsilon III)

**What:** A planet-spanning machine built half a million years ago, still running beneath Epsilon III, sentient-adjacent, capable of time vision and dimensional manipulation, guarded by a lone custodian who cannot leave.
**Where:** The station's own sky.
**Cost of contact:** It chooses operators, not the reverse. It has shown the future; the future took notes.

### 8.2 Babylon 4

**What:** The previous Babylon station, vanished hours after going operational in 2253(?) **[W]**, later confirmed extracted through time six years *backward*, crewed by figures from the last Shadow War. The station that fought a war a millennium ago and is now the Minbari religion's flagship legend.
**Where:** The far past, fully staffed.
**Cost of contact:** Time travel here is a one-way conscription: those who go stay. Any sighting of B4 is either archaeology or prophecy, and no instrument distinguishes them.

### 8.3 Soul Hunters

**What:** An order that captures the souls of the dying at the moment of death, storing them in crystal spheres, believing they preserve greatness, blind to the imprisonment they inflict.
**Where:** Deep-space vessels, solitary.
**Cost of contact:** Proof of afterlife used as a filing system. Their spheres contain witnesses to everything.

### 8.4 Hyperspace Phenomena

**What:** The middle dimension's native hazards, gravitational wakes that shred beacons, "things that live in hyperspace" reported since the first gates, and the static between layers where lost ships drift forever.
**Where:** Everywhere ships travel without realspace reference.
**Cost of contact:** Navigation discipline is life. Every beacon chain has a graveyard at its edge.

### 8.5 The Drafa Plague

**What:** A Markab-origin plague, 100% fatal and transmitted by faith-linked behavior patterns; it erased a species in months before Medlab isolation stopped it aboard.
**Where:** Quarantine archives; any world that hid its dead.
**Cost of contact:** Certainty. The plague killed through the behaviors belief encouraged, epidemiology as theology lesson.

### 8.6 First One Artifacts

**What:** Relics of the departed elder races: jumpgates themselves, the Great Machine class installations, gate-openers that reach places with no names.
**Where:** The Rim, mostly.
**Cost of contact:** Each artifact is a phone line to something that left. Answering is optional; ringing back is not.

### 8.7 Techno-Mages **[E]**

**What:** An order of technologists whose "magic" is sufficiently advanced technology, nanotech familiars, energy cloaks, force blasts, descended from a First One teaching lineage. They withdrew before the Shadow War began, on the principle that their patrons' patrons were about to become obvious.
**Where:** Scattered; gathered once, secretly, when the War started.
**Cost of contact:** They know what's coming and decline to say. Their rule, *do not reveal the technology*, makes them unreliable allies and unforgettable encounters.

### 8.8 Vorlon Encounter Hazard

**What:** Unshielded proximity to an un-suited Vorlon rewrites younger-race perception: each viewer sees a being of their own faith. Glandular contact (poison) enforces the secret.
**Where:** Anywhere Ambassador Kosh walks.
**Cost of contact:** Revelation at the speed of faith. Two people describing the same Vorlon will fight before they compare notes.

| League core worlds ↔ B5 | Varies by gate density | Hours–weeks |
| Rim exploration | Beaconless jump | Not survivable without Elder-race navigation |

**Beacon-chain procedure (off-world ops standard):**

1. Enter hyperspace via gate or ship vortex; acquire nearest beacon signal.
2. Fly the beacon line, deviations measured in meters, not kilometers.
3. At destination gate junction, re-acquire exit beacon before opening vortex.
4. Lost-beacon protocol: broadcast on guard frequencies; wait for rescue tugs. Self-navigation attempts produce the missing-ships lists every port keeps framed.

### Appendix C: Canon Discrepancy Log and Writer's Latitude

| Topic | On-screen variance | Manual ruling |
|---|---|---|
| Station length | "Five miles" stated | 8 km pinned (§2.0) |
| Starfury size/arms | Never precisely stated | §4.1 pins stand |
| Omega spin-jump ritual | Shown repeatedly, never timed | Ruled: rotation stop required before jump/fire integration (§4.1); the ritual is exploitable on-screen |
| Babylon 4 disappearance date | Conflicting references | Logged as discrepancy; use "hours after operational" |
| Shadow ship count | Deliberately vague | Keep vague **[W]** — their numbers are dread, not arithmetic |
| Telepath power scaling | Inconsistent P-levels | P1–P12 scale stands; deep scans are rare and costly |
| Drakh status | Post-pin prominence | Marked [E]/post-pin antagonists (§6.5) |
| Kosh's nature | Intentionally opaque | Remains opaque by ruling — see companion ruling style (SGU §8.0) |

**Writer's latitude principles:** identical to companion volumes, every miracle leaves a bill. Aboard B5 the bills are paid in neutrality spent, favors owed to embassies, telepaths exposed, and history accelerated.
