## PART FOUR-B: SELECTED OBJECT FILES

*Reproduced at summary fidelity. Full procedures reside in the containment database under Level 3/4 seal.*

---

### ITEM #: SCP-173

**Object Class:** Euclid

**Special Containment Procedures:** Item is to be contained within a locked containment cell at all times. Personnel entering are to be briefed and enter in teams of no fewer than three; at least two must maintain direct eye contact with the item until the door is resealed. On any creak or movement within the cell, all personnel are to blink in sequence, never simultaneously.

**Description:** A concrete-and-rebar sculpture animate when line of sight is broken. Movement is instantaneous between observed states. Contact results in death by cervical fracture, followed by [DATA EXPUNGED] from the resulting orifice.

**Addendum, Site-19 incident excerpt:** *"...the two-minute gap in the camera record corresponds exactly to the janitorial staff's coffee break. We do not have a staffing problem. We have a blinking problem."* **[W]**

---

### ITEM #: SCP-096

**Object Class:** Euclid

**Special Containment Procedures:** Item contained in a 5m × 5m × 5m airtight steel cube. No imagery of the face may exist in any medium; web-scrub task forces (MTF Zeta-? under Panopticon oversight) destroy reproductions on discovery. Viewing protocols require personnel to avoid the facial region even in peripheral vision.

**Description:** A pale, emaciated humanoid, 2.38 m tall. When its face is viewed, photographically or directly, the subject enters distress, covers its face, then enters pursuit state: it will travel any distance, through any obstacle, killing the viewer. No force tested has stopped it mid-pursuit.

**Addendum, retrieval interview excerpt (redacted):** *"You didn't look at it. You looked at a picture of it. It doesn't care about the difference, and neither should you."*

---

### ITEM #: SCP-682

**Object Class:** Keter

**Special Containment Procedures:** Item contained in a 5 m acid immersion tank within a 25 m chamber lined with acid-resistant plating. Feeding occurs via chute. Any personnel entering the chamber require Level 4 authorization, full hazmat, and an O5 counter-signature.

**Description:** Large reptilian organism of unknown origin; intelligent, adaptive, and vocal about its contempt for all life. Regenerates from ~1% body mass. Termination attempts (see Incident log: 682-C15 through -C68) have repeatedly enhanced it.

**Addendum:** *"It adapts. Every attempt to end it becomes a lesson it keeps. Recommend we stop teaching."*, Ethics Committee memo, unheeded **[W]**

---

### ITEM #: SCP-049

**Object Class:** Euclid

**Special Containment Procedures:** Humanoid containment suite styled as a 16th-century plague doctor's quarters; interaction by appointment. Subject cooperative if permitted to continue its "work", which is not permitted.

**Description:** Humanoid entity in plague physician garb (garb and body indistinguishable under imaging). Touch is lethal to humans; subjects subsequently reanimate as mindless 049-2 instances. The entity is courteous, articulate, and convinced it is curing a disease only it can perceive.

**Interview excerpt:** *"Good Doctor, your kind calls it murder. I call it the cure arriving before the understanding. History will thank us both, one of us posthumously."*

---

### ITEM #: SCP-914

**Object Class:** Safe

**Special Containment Procedures:** Contained in a climate-controlled 20×20 m room with observation gallery. Testing requires Level 2 sign-off; Very Fine settings require Level 3.

**Description:** Clockwork refinement machine of ~18 m² footprint, comprising an Input booth, Output booth, and a selector dial: **Rough → Coarse → 1:1 → Fine → Very Fine**. Insertion of objects produces refined outputs per inscrutable internal logic.

**Selected test-log extracts:**

| Setting | Input | Output |
|---|---|---|
| 1:1 | 1 kg steel ingot | 1 kg steel shavings |
| Fine | Wristwatch | Better wristwatch |
| Very Fine | Wristwatch | Wristwatch that runs slightly ahead of time. Locally. |
| Rough | Human cadaver | **[DATA EXPUNGED]** — testing suspended |

---

### ITEM #: SCP-106

**Object Class:** Keter

**Special Containment Procedures:** Suspended in a magnetically-levitated containment cell within a lead-lined shell; no solid contact with any surface permitted. Cell breach triggers Protocol 106-Primrose: lure-and-recapture using juvenile human bait **[W]**, Ethics Committee waiver renewed annually over standing objection.

**Description:** Elderly humanoid entity whose passage corrodes matter ("corrosion trails"); phases through solids; drags victims into a pocket dimension of uncertain physics from which return is rare, delayed decades, and psychologically total.

---

## PART SIX: GROUPS OF INTEREST

*Organizations aware of the anomalous. Relations ratings follow Foundation liaison shorthand: positive, neutral, hostile, or hostile-with-overlap.*

### 6.1 Global Occult Coalition (GOC)

**In brief:** United Nations-chartered paramilitary body dedicated to destruction of anomalous objects. Where the Foundation contains, the GOC destroys.

| Property | Detail |
|---|---|
| Structure | UN oversight council + operational strike teams ("PHYSICS division") |
| Doctrine | Anomalies are threats to be eliminated, cataloged only after termination |
| Relations with Foundation | Hostile-with-overlap: shared targets, opposed methods |
| Notable doctrine | The Seventh Standard: no use of anomalous assets, ever |

**Friction point:** GOC destruction of an object the Foundation wants preserved triggers joint-custody standoffs. Their track record includes at least one destroyed object whose termination released something worse **[W]**.

### 6.2 Chaos Insurgency

**In brief:** Former Foundation defectors (the 1924 split) who kept the research and discarded the ethics. They weaponize anomalies instead of containing them, and their cells poach Foundation personnel and inventory by name.

| Property | Detail |
|---|---|
| Origin | Defection of Site-? research elements during the "Insurgency" schism |
| Doctrine | Capture, exploit, sell |
| Relations | Hostile. Active targeting of Foundation assets |

Their knowledge of Foundation procedure makes them the most dangerous human adversary on file: they know our playbook because they wrote part of it.

### 6.3 Serpent's Hand

**In brief:** Decentralized movement asserting the anomalous should be free and public. Operates from the Wanderers' Library (see §8.x), which the Foundation cannot enter by force without consequences nobody can model.

| Property | Detail |
|---|---|
| Structure | Cell-less ideology; no known leadership to capture |
| Doctrine | Liberation of anomalous objects and disclosure to the public |
| Relations | Hostile to Veil enforcement generally; occasionally aligned against worse actors |

**[W]** Serpent's Hand arguments are written to be persuasive. A story where they are simply wrong misses the point of them.

### 6.4 Marshall, Carter & Dark Ltd.

**In brief:** Exclusive club brokering anomalous experiences and objects to the wealthy. They do not breach containment; they purchase inventory through channels we then have to buy back.

| Property | Detail |
|---|---|
| Doctrine | Commerce. Everything has a price list |
| Relations | Neutral-hostile. Litigation-grade rather than violent |

### 6.5 Church of the Broken God

**In brief:** Machine-worship faith seeking reassembly of their broken deity (Mekhane). Anomalous cybernetics, clockwork constructs, and a theology of replacing flesh. Factional splits (Breakers vs. reconstructionists) matter more than the Church's unified front suggests.

### 6.6 Horizon Initiative

Abrahamic-faith umbrella organization claiming religious authority over anomalies. Smaller footprint, significant reach inside faith communities, occasional cooperation on shared containment.

### 6.7 Anderson Robotics

Anomalous robotics and AI vendor. Products circulate through Marshall Carter & Dark channels; their full android line remains unrecovered **[E]**.

### 6.8 Wilson's Wildlife Solutions

Bellingham-area wildlife control that keeps encountering low-grade anomalies and handling them with disarming competence and paperwork the Foundation keeps having to launder. Cooperative. Bring donuts **[E]**.

## PART SEVEN: COMMAND ORGANIZATION

```
O5 COUNCIL (12 seats — identities sealed)
        |
ETHICS COMMITTEE        — authority to veto ANY action, incl. O5 orders
        |
SITE DIRECTORS          — facility command (19, 17, 06-3, etc.)
        |
   +------+----------+--------------+
   |      |          |              |
RESEARCH  SECURITY   MTF COMMAND    RAISA
(divisions per (site security (task force (Records & Information
 anomaly class)  + guards)    deployment)     Security Administration)
```

- **The O5 Council** governs by sealed directive. Council members cannot contact anomalies directly **[W]**.
- **The Ethics Committee** is not a rubber stamp: it exists because unchecked containment produced atrocities the Foundation had to bury twice, once physically and once in the record.
- **RAISA** owns every document you are reading and knows which copies leaked.

---

## PART EIGHT: PHENOMENA BESTIARY

### 8.1 Memetics and Antimemetics

**What:** Memetics: information that behaves like a pathogen, ideas that alter behavior on perception. Kill-agents are offensive memetics. Antimemetics: information that resists being remembered at all; antimemetic anomalies are contained *by forgetting*, which is also how containment staff get lost.
**Where:** Memetics Division, Site-? **[W]**.
**Cost of contact:** You cannot trust your own notes. Antimemetics Division staff leave themselves letters.

### 8.2 Reality Benders

**What:** Individuals or objects that alter consensus reality directly. Type classifications (Blue/Green/Black) scale by power and control.
**Where:** Anywhere. Consensus reality is the battlefield.
**Cost of contact:** Reality benders at scale invalidate containment physics. SRAs level the field; nothing levels it fairly.

### 8.3 Pataphysics **[W]**

**What:** The study of narrative itself as an anomalous force, the hypothesis that our reality is written. Departmental access only.
**Cost of contact:** Departments that research pataphysics report higher rates of authors' block. Draw no conclusions.

### 8.4 Temporal Anomalies

**What:** Loops, reversals, divergent futures, and time-traveling objects. Foundation policy: preserve the timeline unless already broken, in which case document and contain the breakage.
**Cost of contact:** Causality debt. Every fix borrows against a future nobody has audited.

| Canon branches | Mainline vs. canons hub (Broken Masquerade, End of Death...) | This volume pins mainline; branch stories declare their branch |

| 001 proposals | Deliberately plural | All are true-ish; none are confirmed **[W]** — the file's secrecy is the canon |

**Writer's latitude principles:** the Foundation's voice is clinical; its stories are moral. Procedures exist so that when they fail, the failure means something. Do not invent anomalies to pad a scene; the database has thousands, and the best ones were written by someone obeying exactly this constraint.

# VOLUME - DEAD SPACE / CEC

# CONCORDANCE EXTRACTION CORPORATION
## PLANET CRACKER FLEET ASSET DIRECTIVE

*Building a better tomorrow. Restricted distribution.*

*Fifteenth entry in the collected set; house-style variant: authored as CEC internal documentation. Corporate confidence is total; the incidents section is shorter than it should be. [W] notes flag where liability management outruns fact.*

---

## ABOUT THIS DIRECTIVE

This document consolidates fleet specifications, planetary extraction procedures, and personnel policy for Concordance Extraction Corporation assets operating under EarthGov charter. Crew members are reminded that CEC property is the backbone of human civilization, and that resource recovery is not merely an industry but a duty owed to the species.

**Edition pin.** Current as of the **Aegis VII incident review cycle (2508 CE)**. Prior directives superseded. The Corporation expresses condolences to the families involved and reminds all personnel that speculation about causes is both premature and actionable.

**Conventions used throughout:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Verified per Corporate survey |
| **[E]** | Engineering estimate pending field verification |
| **[W]** | Writer's latitude note |

---

## PART ONE: THE COMMERCIAL THEATER

### 1.0 Introduction

Humanity outgrew its cradle and then its solar system. The Resource Wars ended because planet cracking ended scarcity: Concordance Extraction Corporation vessels crack barren planets open and strip them to the core. Every colony breathing easy owes its air to a CEC hull number.

**In brief:** We do not mine planets. We harvest them.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Sol System, EarthGov colonial space, deep-field claims |
| Fleet | Planet Cracker-class vessels (5+ active **[E]**), refinery barges, support craft |
| Regulator | EarthGov: charter oversight, quarantine authority, military arm (USM forces) |
| Market position | CEC is the extraction industry |

Defining facts:

1. **Planet cracking works.** Thirty-five successful cracks aboard the flagship alone. The alternative is the Resource Wars everyone agreed to forget.
2. **EarthGov needs us more than we need EarthGov.** Charter renewals are formalities. Quarantine authority is where they push back.
3. **The Church of Unitology rides our ships.** Their membership includes our crews, our officers, and people above Corporate pay grade. Personnel policy treats faith as private; manifests treat it as data.
### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Era | Event |
|---|---|
| 23rd century | Hideki Ishimura invents the ShockPoint Drive |
| 2214–2300s | Resource Wars over remaining Sol-system reserves |
| 2310s | Planet cracking technique proven on Titan; EarthGov charters CEC |
| 2446, June 9 | USG *Ishimura* commissioned: first Planet Cracker-class hull, first capital ship with ShockPoint drive |
| 2446–2508 | 34 successful planet cracks; colonies flourish; Church of Unitology grows aboard CEC crews |
| 2497 | Wanat Disaster: gravity tether failure destroys a Planet Cracker and its harvest site |
| 2508 | Aegis VII: illegal planet crack; Red Marker recovered; colony and flagship lost. **Edition pin** |

### 1.3 Assessment of Powers

**Concordance Extraction Corporation.** The extraction monopoly. Fleet, foundries, colonial infrastructure contracts, and the political weight of feeding humanity.

**EarthGov.** Colonial federal authority: military (USM forces), quarantine power, and a standing interest in anything that threatens the species. EarthGov warships outrank Corporate security by design.

**Church of Unitology.** Fastest-growing faith in human space. Doctrine centers on the Markers as divine instruments and "Convergence" as salvation. The Church's reach inside CEC crews is extensive, well-funded, and documented in manifests the Corporation does not audit **[W]**.

**The Marker situation.** See Part Six §6.1. Personnel are reminded that unauthorized discussion of Marker-related phenomena violates standing quarantine directives.

### 1.5 Status Quo Snapshot (Aegis VII Review Cycle)

| Domain | Status |
|---|---|
| Aegis VII | Quarantined under EarthGov blockade; colony lost |
| USG Ishimura | Adrift at Aegis VII; contact lost; salvage/recovery pending Corporate decision |
| Fleet morale | Strained; Unitology recruitment rising fleet-wide |
| Corporate posture | Incident attributed to piracy and sabotage pending final review **[W]** |
| EarthGov posture | Military quarantine enforced; investigation ongoing |

## PART TWO: THE USG ISHIMURA

### 2.0 Vessel Overview

**In brief:** The first Planet Cracker-class vessel and still the largest: one mile of industrial ship named for the inventor of the ShockPoint Drive, sixty-three years in service, thirty-five cracks completed. The pride of the CEC fleet.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Class | Planet Cracker-class mining vessel |
| Length | 5,300 ft (1.6 km) |
| Commissioned | June 9, 2446 |
| Complement | 1,332 (see breakdown below) |
| FTL | ShockPoint Drive (first capital-scale fit) |
| Armament | Asteroid Defense System cannons (mining-grade) |
| Fate | Adrift, Aegis VII system, 2508 |

**Complement breakdown (manifest):**

| Department | Headcount |
|---|---|
| Technical | 177 |
| Colonial liaison | 307 |
| Service/janitorial | 145 + 88 custodial |
| Security | 89 |
| Medical | 69 |
| Clerical | 65 |
| Crew officers | 80 |
| Extension/familial (dependents) | 208 |
| Other | 104 |
### 2.01 Deck Plan (operational zones)

| Deck | Function | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Bridge | Command, navigation, tether coordination | Captain Mathius's authority ends here |
| Flight Deck | Hangar bays, shuttle operations, ADS gunnery | Primary boarding point |
| Crew Deck | Quarters, mess, executive suites, Church meeting space **[W]** | |
| Medical Deck | Autodoc clinics, research wing (Marker study **[W]**) | Research access by EarthGov/Church pressure |
| Hydroponics | Food production, air recycling | Life-support keystone |
| Mining Deck | Ore processing, gravity tethers, fracture array control, value storage | The reason the ship exists |
| Engineering | ShockPoint drive, fusion reactors, fuel storage | Radiation-suited crews only |
| Maintenance | Crawlspace infrastructure between all decks | Unmonitored; the ship's blind lymph system |

### 2.02 Planet Crack Procedure

1. Survey team confirms target viability (barren rock preferred; colony clearance required for inhabited claims).
2. Colony installs gravity tethers at the harvest seam.
3. Fracture array fires: a planet-sized chunk is broken from the body.
4. Tethers haul the chunk into orbit and into the Mining Deck's maw.
5. Onboard processing strips ore; refined stock loads to cargo.

**Failure modes:** tether cascade failure (Wanat Disaster, 2497: total asset loss), orbital decay under load, and fracture-seam misreads. Corporate review notes that prior to Aegis VII only one operation in three dozen met with less-than-optimal results. The review does not count Aegis VII as an extraction failure, because the rock came up fine **[W]**.

---

## PART THREE: SHOCKPOINT OPERATIONS AND COMMUNICATIONS

**ShockPoint Drive:** FTL transit via shockpoint jumps. Range and precision are proven technology; jump calculations are computer-assisted. First capital-scale fit aboard Ishimura (2446).

**Communications:** EarthGov Relay network handles interstellar traffic. Ships may declare radio silence; CEC policy permits it for operational security, and Aegis VII demonstrated how a silent flagship becomes a legal fiction until someone investigates **[W]**.

## PART FIVE: EQUIPMENT AND PERSONNEL SYSTEMS

### 5.1 The RIG (Resource Integration Gear)

Every crew member wears a RIG: a full-body suit with holographic spine display showing health status, stasis energy, and air reserve. Suit integrity, weapon ammo counts, and credits project into the wearer's vision. The RIG is why headcount after incidents is precise even when survivors are not.

### 5.2 Tools as Weapons (Corporate Policy Notice)

CEC vessels do not carry military weapons. Personnel defend themselves with mining equipment:

| Tool | Original function | Field use |
|---|---|---|
| Plasma Cutter | Precision ore slicing | The standard; vertical/horizontal fire mode |
| Line Gun | Wide-beam cutting | Area denial; mines |
| Pulse Rifle | Security model (P-Sec) | Automatic fire; issued to security only |
| Ripper | Cable-saw blade projection | Devastating at close range |
| Flamethrower | Debris clearing | Swarm suppression |

### 5.3 Stasis and Kinesis Modules

Engineering-grade modules integrated into RIGs:

- **Stasis module:** emits a field slowing objects and machinery for seconds. Rated for industrial accidents. Also rated, as it turns out, against fast-moving biological hazards **[E]**.
- **Kinesis module:** telekinetic grapple for moving heavy salvage. Range: short. Utility: endless.

**[W]** The tools-as-weapons doctrine is the franchise's voice: nothing aboard was designed to fight what the crew faces, and improvisation is the only arsenal that ships on time.
---

## PART SIX: SECTION 7: BIOHAZARD AND PHENOMENA DOSSIER

*(This section exists because Section 6 was sealed by Corporate review. The numbering is retained so auditors know where to look.)*

### 6.1 The Markers

**In brief:** Alien obelisks emitting a signal field that influences living minds: dementia, hallucination, compulsion, and religious revelation in exposed populations. Black Marker (original, Earth-origin discovery predating the Corporation) and Red Markers (copies) share the signal profile.

**Corporate position:** Markers are mineralogical curiosities of high research value. Recovery operations are authorized when profitable.

**Field reality **[W]**:** exposure cascades through a population as insomnia, violence, mass hysteria, and faith. The Church of Unitology teaches that Markers are divine and their signal is holy. The Church may be right. That possibility is the problem.

### 6.2 Necromorphs

**In brief:** Recombinant dead. The Marker signal restructures necrotic tissue into hostile organisms from whatever corpses are available: crew, patients, family members. Necromorphs are not infected people; they are the corpses of infected people, rebuilt into tools of slaughter.

| Variant | Built from | Threat profile |
|---|---|---|
| Slasher | Single corpse + blade-limb grafts | Standard attacker; fast, flanking |
| Infector | Winged baton-form | Converts fresh corpses mid-battle; priority target |
| Leaper | Limb-optimized recombination | Wall-crawling, lunging |
| Brute | Massed biomass | Charging mini-boss; armor-plated front |
| Pregnant | Swollen carrier | Releases swarms when ruptured **[W]** |
| Guardians / Swarmers | Wall-integrated tissue | Area denial |

**Kill doctrine (mandatory training point):** Necromorphs ignore headshots. Dismemberment stops them: sever the limbs. This single fact separates survivors from statistics, and it is the reason CEC cutting tools outperform military rifles aboard its own ships.

### 6.3 Convergence

**What:** The Marker end-state: mass death followed by mass recombination into something planetary-scale. Aegis VII did not reach Convergence. The Church believes Convergence is salvation.
**Where:** Wherever enough dead meet an active Marker.
**Cost of contact:** Everything the word "everything" covers.

### 6.4 The Church of Unitology (institutional dossier)

A faith founded on a recovered Marker artifact a century before Aegis VII. Structure: centralized church leadership, congregation cells inside every major institution including CEC crews. Doctrine: Markers are the divine mechanism; death in their service is transformation.

The Church's interference in the Aegis VII response is documented in sealed appendices. Personnel policy prohibits discrimination by faith. Manifest policy records it anyway **[W]**.

---

## PART SEVEN: CORPORATE AND GOVERNMENT ORGANIZATION

```
EARTHGOV                    — colonial federal authority
        |
CONCORDANCE EXTRACTION CORPORATION — charter holder
        |
   +--------+----------------+
   |        |                |
FLEET OPS  PLANETARY DIV    RESEARCH DIV
(crackers, (colony setup,    (Marker studies,
 barges)    tether crews)     medical R&D)
   |
SHIPBOARD CHAIN: Captain → Deck Chiefs → Crew → Contract Labor
```

- **EarthGov quarantine authority** overrides Corporate commerce during declared emergencies. The Aegis VII blockade is the test case everyone is watching.
- **USM military forces** answer to EarthGov, not CEC. Their appearance at a "training exercise" near your incident site is information **[W]**.
- **Crew governance:** civilian merchant law applies aboard; captains hold police power; unions hold grievance power. Both matter after an incident, when blame gets assigned in depositions.

---

## PART EIGHT: HAZARD BESTIARY

### 8.0 Marker Dementia

**What:** Progressive psychological deterioration in Marker-exposed populations: insomnia, irritability, hallucinations (dead loved ones are common), homicidal episodes, religious conversion.
**Where:** Anywhere a Marker broadcasts.
**Cost of contact:** The dementia is functional. It moves exposed populations toward killing each other so raw material accumulates near the Marker. The disease is the harvest mechanism.

### 8.2 Gravity Tether Cascade

**What:** Industrial failure mode: tether loss under load drops a planet-cracked fragment back onto the surface below.
**Where:** Extraction sites (Wanat, 2497).
**Cost of contact:** Extinction-of-site scale. Tether inspections are mandatory, logged, and the first thing corner-cutting cuts.

### 8.3 ShockPoint Misjump

**What:** Navigation errors in shockpoint transit strand vessels off-course or damage drive systems irreparably.
**Where:** Deep-space lanes.
**Cost of contact:** Rescue is a business decision. CEC crews update their wills accordingly.

### 8.4 Unitology Aboard Ship

**What:** Not a hazard per Corporate policy. In practice: unregistered Church meetings during quarantines, refusal of medical procedures conflicting with doctrine, and crew loyalty networks that outrank deck chiefs.
**Cost of contact:** Unknown until it is everyone **[W]**.

---

# VOLUME - PACIFIC RIM - PPDC

*Eve of Operation Pitfall 2025*

---

# PAN PACIFIC DEFENSE CORPS
## JAEGER ACADEMY FIELD MANUAL

**CANCEL THE APOCALYPSE**

*Sixteenth entry in the collected set; house-style variant: authored as a PPDC Ranger Academy training text. Its voice is drill-sergeant practical.*

---

## ABOUT THIS MANUAL

This text trains Rangers in Jaeger operations, drift protocol, and Kaiju engagement. Every procedure in it was written in blood during the fifteen years the Breach has been open.

**Edition pin.** Current as of **the eve of Operation Pitfall (2025 CE)**: the assault on the Breach itself.

**Conventions:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Verified per PPDC records |
| **[E]** | Estimate; shatterdome variance applies |
| **[W]** | Writer's latitude note |

---

## PART ONE — THE KAIJU WAR

### 1.0 Introduction

The first Kaiju came through a portal at the bottom of the Pacific and took six days and three cities' worth of ordnance to kill. It took humanity one look at the corpse's biology to understand two things: these things were engineered, and more were coming. The Jaeger Program is humanity's answer: giant mecha, piloted by two minds fused into one, deployed from coastal fortresses against monsters that scale up every year.

**In brief:** The war is simple. Something on the other side of the ocean floor sends increasingly large creatures through a door. We meet them with robots the size of buildings.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Pacific Rim coastline; Breach at Challenger Deep |
| Command | Pan Pacific Defense Corps (PPDC) |
| Assets | Active Shatterdomes (Hong Kong primary at pin date); Jaeger fleet reduced to four |
| Enemy | Kaiju: Category I–V bio-weapons; Precursor architects behind the Breach |

Defining facts:

1. **Kaiju scale escalates by category.** Category I tested us. Category IV beats whole fleets. Category V exists.
2. **Jaeger production lost to Wall of Life politics.** The UN defunded the program for coastal walls; walls do not punch back.
3. **The Breach is genetic-keyed.** Only Kaiju DNA passes both ways. Closing it requires taking a nuclear payload *through* it, which requires getting there alive.
### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Date | Event |
|---|---|
| Aug 2013 | Trespasser (Category I) makes landfall at San Francisco. Six days, three cities, nuclear kill. The Breach opens the war |
| 2014–2016 | Kaiju attacks accelerate; Jaeger Program founded; first Marks roll out |
| Jul 2017 | Gipsy Danger launched (Mark-3, USA); Yamarashi killed in Los Angeles |
| 2019–2020s | Mark-1 through Mark-5 generations; kills outpace attacks, briefly |
| 2020, Feb 29 | Knifehead vs Gipsy Danger, Gulf of Alaska: Yancy Becket killed; solo-pilot survival proves drift damage is real |
| 2024–2025 | UN defunds program for the Anti-Kaiju Wall; shatterdomes close; four Jaegers remain at Hong Kong |
| 2025 | Double-event on Hong Kong (Leatherback + Otachi); **Operation Pitfall approved. Edition pin: eve of assault** |

### 1.3 Assessment of Forces

**The PPDC.** Multinational military science organization: academies (Kodiak Island), shatterdomes, LOCCENT mission control, K-Science division (kaiju biology), J-Tech (Jaeger engineering).

**The Precursors.** The enemy's engineers: trans-dimensional xenobiologists engineering Kaiju as invasion weapons, category by category, generation by generation. They are patient, they are winning the attrition math, and they are not present on our side of the Breach.

**Kaiju Blue.** Every kill contaminates: Kaiju blood is toxic cyan-blue and vaporizes into an airborne blight when carelessly spilled. A perfect kill poisons a city anyway. Cleanup doctrine exists; "perfect" does not.
---

## PART TWO — DRIFT PROTOCOL AND SHATTERDOME OPERATIONS

### 2.0 The Drift (Neural Handshake)

**In brief:** Jaegers are too large for one nervous system. Two Rangers pilot as one through the Drift: a neural handshake merging motor control, memory, and instinct. Left hemisphere and right hemisphere of one giant brain.

**Rules of the Drift:**

1. **Do not chase the R.A.B.I.T.** Random Access Brain Impulse Triggers: memories that surface mid-drift. Chasing them seizes the connection. Let them pass.
2. **Familiarity deepens the bond.** Sibling pairs and long partnerships drift deeper. Loss in the pod is loss in the mind.
3. **Solo piloting is possible once.** The neural load nearly kills the pilot. It has been done twice on record. Both pilots earned legends; neither volunteered again lightly.
4. **Kaiju brains can be drifted into.** K-Science uses single-pilot drift with extracted Kaiju tissue to read the Precursor network. This is the bravest and least advisable thing anyone does in this war.

### 2.1 LOCCENT Mission Control

Local command center: tracks Kaiju signatures from the Breach approach, drops Jaegers by helicopter sling, coordinates with civilian authorities, and calls the fight. The Marshal commands from LOCCENT or suits up personally; both have precedent.

---

## PART THREE — JAEGER ENGAGEMENT DOCTRINE

1. **Fight at the waterline.** Kaiju are amphibious but clumsy ashore; Jaeger armor is thickest for coastal brawl.
2. **Plasmacaster first, blades last.** Kaiju blood is toxic: cutting wounds the city. Plasma burns cauterize.
3. **Dismemberment over headshots.** Kaiju biology distributes function; decapitation is not assumed lethal.
4. **Category discipline:** engagement authorization scales with category. Category IV requires multi-Jaeger deployment. Category V had no doctrine until Slattern.
5. **Nuclear option:** the Breach closes to Kaiju genetics only. A nuclear payload delivered *through* it is the only confirmed closure method.

---

## PART FOUR — THE JAEGER ROSTER

### 4.1 Gipsy Danger (Mark-3, USA)

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Launched | July 10, 2017 |
| Height / Weight | 79.25 m / 1,980 tons |
| Ratings | Speed 7 · Strength 8 · Armor 6 |
| Power | Nuclear Vortex Turbine (analog Arc-9 reactor): **EMP-immune**, unlike digital Mark-IV/V suites |
| Weapons | I-19 Plasmacaster ×2 · GD6 Chain Sword (retractable) |
| Kills | 9 |

The last analog Jaeger and the war's most famous hull. Rebuilt after Knifehead under the Mark-III Restoration Program (solid iron hull, no alloys). Destroyed at the Breach: self-destruct payload delivered, Slattern dragged down with her **[W]**.

### 4.2 Striker Eureka (Mark-5, Australia)

Fastest Jaeger ever built; eleven kills, the record. Twin chest-launched missiles (K-Stunner), brass-knuckle thrusters. Digital suite: grounded by Leatherback's EMP at Hong Kong. Piloted by the Hansen father-son team.

### 4.3 Cherno Alpha (Mark-1, Russia)

Oldest active Jaeger at pin date: first-generation hull, Tesla fists incinerating at contact, cylinder-head design built like a nuclear plant containment. Crewed by the Kaidanovskys. Lost holding the Hong Kong perimeter against Leatherback and Otachi. Her stand bought the city its evacuation.

### 4.4 Crimson Typhoon (Mark-4, China)

Triple-arm configuration, three pilots (Wei Tang triplets), Thundercloud Formation technique, sawblade limbs. Lost at Hong Kong to Otachi's ambush; plasma chain cannon outranged before close work could matter.

### 4.5 Coyote Tango (Mark-1, Japan)

Cannons-only design for pilots unsuited to melee (radiation history); Stacker Pentecost solo-piloted her against Venom Osaka-scale events. Decommissioned before pin date; his radiation ledger is why.

---

## PART FIVE — WEAPONS AND SYSTEMS

| System | Notes |
|---|---|
| Plasmacaster | Deployable plasma cannon; cauterizes Kaiju Blue risk |
| Chain sword | Retractable blade; last-resort (blood hazard) |
| Rocket punch | Elbow-thrust kinetic strike (Gipsy signature) |
| Nuclear Vortex Turbine | Analog power core; doubles as point-blank burn weapon |
| Tesla fists | Cherno Alpha: contact incineration |
| Escape pods | Conn-Pod ejects; survival rate is honest, not good |

**Ranger physiology:** daily Metharocin doses manage drift side effects. Post-combat: neural scans mandatory. The Academy screens for drift compatibility before anything else, because talent without compatibility is a funeral with extra steps.

---

## PART SIX — THREAT DOSSIERS

### 6.0 Kaiju Classification

| Category | Threat scale | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| I | City nuisance; early war | Trespasser-class |
| II | Serious; requires coordinated response | Yamarashi |
| III | Major engagement; Jaeger-standard opponent | Knifehead |
| IV | Multi-Jaeger threat; EMP/bio special weapons | Leatherback, Otachi, Raiju |
| V | Never seen before Slattern; fleet-scale | Slattern |

Escalation pattern: each category arrives faster than the last. The enemy is iterating.

### 6.1 Kaiju Biology and Behavior

Engineered organisms sharing a genetic signature across categories: grown, not born wild. Behavior shows reconnaissance (early attacks probed defenses), adaptation (counter-weapons appear two attacks later), and coordination (paired assaults). Otachi carried an unborn infant Kaiju; pregnancy was new. Assume the next surprise is already in production.

### 6.2 The Precursors

Trans-dimensional civilization behind the Breach: xenobiologists terraforming Earth by extermination. They see this as scheduled development, not war. Their timetable accelerated when humanity started winning; assume their next iteration is worse than Slattern.

---

## PART SEVEN — PPDC COMMAND ORGANIZATION

```
UNITED NATIONS (funding — contested)
        |
PPDC HIGH COMMAND       — Marshal-grade authority per region
        |
SHATTERDOME COMMAND     — Marshal Pentecost (Hong Kong at pin)
        |
   +------+------------+----------------+
   |      |            |                |
LOCCENT   J-TECH      K-SCIENCE         RANGER ACADEMY
(mission   (Jaeger     (kaiju biology,   (Kodiak Island;
 control)   engineering) drift research)   drift training)
```

- **Marshal authority:** operational command of Jaeger deployment; political authority varies by host nation and erodes under Wall-of-Life politics.
- **Ranger corps:** volunteer elite; drift-compatible pairs trained at Kodiak. Recruitment post-defund runs on stubbornness.
- **K-Science division:** kaiju biology research; drifting with kaiju tissue remains its highest-risk instrument.

---

## PART EIGHT — WAR BESTIARY

### 8.0 The Breach

**What:** Trans-dimensional portal at Challenger Deep, genetic-keyed to Kaiju DNA.
**Where:** Pacific floor.
**Cost of contact:** Conventional ordnance bounces off its field. Nuclear delivery through the throat of the thing is the only confirmed closure mechanism, and it requires surviving everything between here and there.

### 8.2 Kaiju Blue Contamination

**What:** Toxic blood event: airborne cyan blight from ruptured kaiju biology.
**Where:** Every kill site.
**Cost of contact:** Cities quarantine districts for months. Blades make it worse; plasma makes it better.

### 8.3 The Drift's Dark Corner

**What:** Memories in the drift can be weapons: a partner who knows your worst day owns your focus during combat. Precursor-influenced minds inside a drift are a standing nightmare scenario.
**Cost of contact:** Trust is equipment. Maintain it like a reactor.

### 8.4 The Wall of Life

**What:** UN-funded coastal barriers replacing Jaeger deployment.
**Cost of contact:** Walls do not punch back. Sydney demonstrated what a Category IV thinks of walls: it climbed over. The program's revival is the pin-date's only good news.

---

# VOLUME - FALLOUT / VAULT-TEC

*Commonwealth - pin 2287*

---

# VAULT-TEC CORPORATION
## VAULT OPERATIONS MANUAL: OCCUPANT EDITION

*Sixteenth entry... correction: a foundational text of the collected set; house-style variant: authored as pre-War corporate literature. The Corporation's optimism is sincere policy. The **[W]** notes tell you what the policy was actually for.*

---

## ABOUT THIS MANUAL

Congratulations, valued applicant! You have been selected for residency in a Vault-Tec underground shelter: the last word in post-nuclear living. This manual covers facility operations, occupant responsibilities, and the American can-do spirit that will see our great nation through any unpleasantness.

**Document status.** Printed 2077 CE, weeks before the Great War (October 23, 2077; duration: approximately two hours). Distribution: selected vaults. This copy carries post-War annotation markers (**[W]**) added by surviving archivists.

**Conventions:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Official Vault-Tec policy |
| **[E]** | Engineering estimate; vault variance applies |
| **[W]** | Archivist note: read carefully |

---

## PART ONE: THE AMERICAN DREAM, UNDERGROUND

### 1.0 Introduction

In the event of total atomic annihilation, Vault-Tec shelters preserve America's greatest asset: its people. Each vault is a self-contained civilization rated for indefinite underground habitation. Your Overseer is your mayor, your employer, and your guardian. Trust the Overseer. Trust Vault-Tec.

**[W]** The Vaults were never shelters first. Vault-Tec contracted with shadow clients to run *social experiments* on sealed populations: 122 public vaults, each with a variable, most with a twist. The door was real. The purpose on the brochure was not.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation (Post-War)

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Post-nuclear North American continent ("the Wasteland") |
| Population centers | Vaults (sealed or opened), settlements, ruins |
| Powers | New California Republic (expansionist democracy), Brotherhood of Steel (tech-hoarding knightly order), Caesar's Legion (slaver empire, East), raider bands, independent towns |
| Hazards | Radiation, mutated fauna, Super Mutants, Feral Ghouls, raiders, and every Vault that opened wrong |

Three facts define the Wasteland:

1. **The bombs were the beginning, not the end.** Two hours of war created two centuries of consequences.
2. **Pre-War technology is the treasure map.** Power armor, energy weapons, and working Vaults decide who rules.
3. **Every Vault opened tells you what Vault-Tec thought people were for.**
### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Date | Event |
|---|---|
| 2050s | Resource Wars begin; Europe/Middle East nuclear exchange; United States annexes Canada |
| 2066–2077 | Sino-American War; Anchorage Reclamation (2077, T-51b power armor decisive) |
| 2077, Oct 23 | **The Great War.** Two hours of exchange. Vault doors close |
| 2090s–2160s | First vaults open (Vault 13's water crisis forces the surface); Shady Sands founded; Master's Super Mutant army rises and falls |
| 2186 | New California Republic founded from Shady Sands |
| 2200s–2287 | Brotherhood of Steel expansion; Enclave remnant wars; East Coast developments; the Commonwealth's Institute revealed; **Edition pin: 2287, the Sole Survivor emerges from Vault 111** |

### 1.3 Assessment of Powers

**New California Republic (NCR).** The Wasteland's largest democracy: taxes, laws, an army, and all the growing pains of old America replayed at survival scale.

**Brotherhood of Steel.** Technology-preservation knightly order: power armor, vertibirds, and a catechism about who deserves advanced tech (answer: them).

**The Institute (Commonwealth).** Post-War boogeyman made real: synths (biosynthetic replicants), teleportation, and a scientific council deciding humanity's replacement schedule **[W]**.

**Raider bands & Super Mutants.** The Wasteland's weather: seasonal, violent, survivable with walls.

### 1.5 Status Quo Snapshot (2287 CE)

| Domain | Status |
|---|---|
| Commonwealth (Boston) | Institute exposed; Railroad/Minutemen/Brotherhood factions in open conflict over synth policy |
| NCR | Continental republic stretching from the Boneyard to the Mojave |
| Vault legacy | Most vaults opened, failed, or became towns; sealed ones remain unopened stories |
| Pre-War tech | Power armor and energy cells still decide battles |

---

## PART TWO: VAULT OPERATIONS

### 2.0 Facility Overview

**In brief:** A standard Vault is a self-contained underground township: 50–1,000 occupant capacity **[E]**, gear-door blast seal rated for direct nuclear strike, closed-cycle air/water/power, hydroponics farms, and a social order run by an Overseer with absolute authority and a sealed envelope of instructions from Vault-Tec.

```
   STANDARD VAULT — SECTION CUT (schematic)

   [SURFACE] gear-shaped blast door on hydraulic arms
      || entrance corridor + decon showers
   ____||____________________________
   | ATRIUM: civic center · shops     |
   | RESIDENTIAL: dormitory wings     |
   | CLINIC · SCIENCE LABS            |
   | CAFETERIA · WATER PROCESSING     |
   | REACTOR HALL (fusion)            |
   | OVERSEER'S OFFICE (sealed files) |
   |__________________________________|
        [ATOMIC COFFEE SHOP OPEN 24HRS]
```

### 2.01 Occupant Systems

- **Pip-Boy 3000 (Personal Information Processor):** wrist-mounted computer issued to every resident: stats, inventory, radio (Vault-Tec broadcasts only, pre-War stations persist as static and miracles), map display.
- **G.O.A.T. (Generalized Occupational Aptitude Test):** career assignment exam taken at age 16. Your result decides your job for life. Retesting discouraged **[W]**.
- **Rations & water:** processed from recycled systems; quality is a budget line the Overseer manages.
- **Power armor displays & energy weapons stay in the armory**, which stays Overseer-locked. See §2.02 for why some vaults regretted that.
### 2.02 The Vault Experiment Program **[W: this is the manual's dark heart]**

**Archivist's note:** The brochure said "shelter." The contracts with the Enclave said "laboratory." Most vaults were social experiments with human populations as instruments. Selected entries from the Vault-Tec experiment registry:

| Vault | Official purpose | Actual variable |
|---|---|---|
| Vault 13 | Standard shelter | Water chip lifespan deliberately short: forced surface contact on a schedule |
| Vault 101 | Standard shelter | Door never opens. Ever. Overseer succession by any means |
| Vault 111 | Cryogenic preservation | Cryo pods for select residents; everyone else was furniture |
| Vault 112 | Virtual reality refuge | Occupants locked in a simulation run by a madman |
| Vault 108 | Cloning facility | Population of clones, all named Gary |
| Vault 11 | Civic duty | Annual sacrifice election: refuse and the vault punishes everyone |
| Vault 75 | Education | Children processed for "genetic improvement"; adults did not survive intake |
| Vault 95 | Rehabilitation | Chem-addicts cured, then offered the drugs again at peak misery |
| Vault 106 | Standard shelter | Hallucinogenic gas in the air supply |
| Vault 118 | Luxury suites | Ghoulish residents; power ran to the wrong floors |

The control vaults (e.g., Vault 76) existed to be comparisons: opened on schedule, no variable. Their populations became the Wasteland's founding stock.

**[W]** Every vault is a short story premise: a sealed society, one twisted variable, and a door that opens when the data is complete. The horror is bureaucratic.

---

## PART THREE: WASTELAND SURVIVAL OPERATIONS

### 3.1 Radiation Discipline

- Rad-Away (chelating IV) and Rad-X (pre-exposure chem) are survival currency.
- Irradiated water heals nothing and feeds everything: drink filtered or suffer mutation risk (ghouls are the endpoint of chronic exposure, not a guarantee).
- Geiger counters are standard Pip-Boy attachments. Trust the clicking.

### 3.2 Threat Profile (Wasteland Standard)

| Threat | Profile | Doctrine |
|---|---|---|
| Feral ghouls | Radiation-zombified humans, swarm attacks | Headshots work; melee risks infection bites |
| Super Mutants | FEV-transformed giants, organized bands | Heavy weapons; avoid their camps |
| Raiders | Human predators in tribes | Treat as armed and creative |
| Deathclaws | Apex bio-weapon fauna | Avoidance is the doctrine. Really |
| Radroaches/Mirelurks/Bloatflies | Mutant fauna | Protein source with proper cooking |
---

## PART FOUR: PRE-WAR TECHNOLOGY REGISTRY

| Technology | Function | Wasteland status |
|---|---|---|
| T-45/T-51b/T-60 Power Armor | Servo-assisted nuclear-powered armor | Brotherhood/NCR/Enclave crown jewels; fusion-core powered |
| Laser/Ion weaponry | Energy small arms and heavy platforms | Scarcity drives salvage wars |
| GECK (Garden of Eden Creation Kit) | Terrain/soil/agriculture terraforming suite | Legend-grade; a few confirmed functional |
| Pip-Boy 3000 | Personal information processor | Vault-issue; collectors kill for them |
| Vertibird | VTOL gunship transport | Enclave/Brotherhood/NCR air power |
| Protectron/Mr. Handy robots | Labor automatons | Still running pre-War routes two centuries later |
| FEV (Forced Evolutionary Virus) | Super Mutant source | The Institute's ongoing sin **[W]** |

---

## PART FIVE: FACTIONS AND DIPLOMACY

### 5.1 Brotherhood of Steel

Tech-preservation military order descended from pre-War Army elements. Doctrine: advanced technology in untrained hands caused the Great War; the Brotherhood collects it like fire crews collect matches. Ranks mirror knightly orders (Initiate → Elder). Their air fleet and power armor make them a superpower with a monastery's population.

### 5.2 The Institute (Commonwealth dossier)

Post-War scientific underground beneath Boston: teleportation, synths (Generation 3 biosynthetic humans indistinguishable from people), and a governing council that replaced surface humanity's future with its own design program. Synth liberation politics are the Commonwealth's civil rights war **[W]**.

### 5.3 New California Republic

Democratic federation: elected president, two-house legislature, taxation, and an army fighting old America's problems at new-America scale. The NCR proves democracy survives the bomb, and that it inherits every flaw democracy ever had.

---

## PART SIX: HAZARD BESTIARY

### 6.0 The Great War Legacy

**What:** Two hours of exchange left 77 years of winter-less radiation seasons, dead oceans, and continents of ruins holding live warheads.
**Cost of contact:** Chronic everywhere. Rad-X is a meal planning decision.

### 6.2 FEV and Super Mutants

**What:** Forced Evolutionary Virus converting humans into sterile giants of varying intelligence; the Master's army (West) and the Institute's experiments (East) both manufacture them.
**Cost of contact:** Conversion is irreversible. The mutants remember being human, sometimes.

### 6.3 Ghoul Continuum

**What:** Radiation-scarred humans: feral ghouls (mindless aggression) retain memory fragments; normal ghouls (centenarian survivors) are fully sapient citizens facing two centuries of prejudice.
**Cost of contact:** Prejudice, mostly. Ferals bite.

### 6.4 The Enclave Question **[W]**

**What:** The pre-War government's continuity-of-command remnant: pure-blood doctrine, vertibird fleets, and genocidal filtration plans for "mutie" populations.
**Status:** Defeated twice, never quite gone.

---

## PART SEVEN: VAULT CULTURE AND GOVERNANCE

- **The Overseer:** executive authority per charter; experiment vaults gave the role sealed directives from Corporate. The tension between Overseer instructions and occupant survival is every vault's founding drama.
- **Succession:** appointment, election, assassination, or cloning, depending on vault. Document your succession before someone documents it for you.
- **Opening day:** the moment a vault recontacts the Wasteland is its graduation and its exposure. Some thrived (76, 8), some collapsed on the threshold (13 nearly), some should never have opened at all (112 was already too late).

---

## APPENDICES

### Appendix A: Glossary

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| G.O.A.T. | Generalized Occupational Aptitude Test |
| GECK | Garden of Eden Creation Kit |
| Overseer | Vault commander |
| Pip-Boy 3000 | Wrist-mounted personal computer |
| Rads | Radiation dose units |
| The Great War | October 23, 2077 nuclear exchange |
| Vault-Tec Corporation | Shelter manufacturer/experiment authority |

### Appendix B: Survival Metrics

| Resource | Standard |
|---|---|
| Water ration | 1 L/day filtered (Rad-Away reserve separate) |
| Caps (currency) | Nuka-Cola bottle caps: backed by water/purification scarcity |
| Ammunition | Hand-loaded preferred; energy cells scarce |

### Appendix C: Discrepancy Log and Writer's Latitude

| Topic | Variance across games | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| Canon protagonist events | Player-variable outcomes | Regional snapshots pin only fixed canon; player choices flagged **[W]** |
| Vault experiment list | Expands each game | Registry entries here are canonical examples; new vaults may exist |
| Power armor generations | Stats vary by title | T-45 < T-51 < X-01 lineage ordering stands |
| Ghoul biology | Inconsistent by design | Radiation continuum ruling (§6.3) |

**Writer's latitude principles:** the satire IS the setting. Every cheerful corporate sentence should have its invoice somewhere in the Wasteland.

---

*End of manual. Thank you for choosing Vault-Tec. Please return your tray table to its upright position and remember: the door opens when the data is complete.*

| PREY - TALOS I | Lunar orbit - outbreak 2032 |


<!-- ====== VOLUME: PREY - TALOS I ====== -->

# VOLUME - PREY - TALOS I

*Lunar orbit - outbreak 2032*

---

# TRANSTAR INDUSTRIES
## TALOS I STATION OPERATIONS MANUAL

*The future is ours to build.*

*Seventeenth entry in the collected set; house-style variant: authored as TranStar corporate documentation. Polished optimism throughout; the incident appendices are thinner than the incident reports they summarize.*

---

## ABOUT THIS MANUAL

This manual orients new personnel aboard Talos I, TranStar Industries' premier orbital research station: home of the Neuromod program, the Typhon research initiative, and humanity's most valuable patent portfolio.

**Edition pin.** Current as of **the station-wide emergency declared during the annual shareholder demonstration (2032 CE)**. Subsequent events are under review.

---

## PART ONE: ORBITAL THEATER

### 1.0 Introduction

Talos I orbits the Moon where a Cold War satellite defense platform once watched for Soviet missiles. TranStar rebuilt it around something stranger: an alien structure recovered from deep space, older than human civilization, which we did not build and cannot fully enter. Everything valuable aboard grew from studying it.

**In brief:** The station is a laboratory built around its own experiment, staffed by people who forgot the experiment predates them.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Lunar orbit |
| Complement | ~500 personnel: scientists, support staff, security **[E]** |
| Assets | Neuromod patents, Typhon specimens, psychotronics research |
| Contingency | Self-destruct protocol requires CEO authorization and reactor override |

Defining facts:

1. **Neuromods are the product.** Instant skill acquisition from injectable Typhon-derived material: surgery plus memory integration. One neuromod makes you a marksman, a hacker, a surgeon.
2. **Typhon research outranks safety culture** when quarterly results are due **[W]**.
3. **Earth is one infected shuttle away.** Station containment failures are planetary threats by definition.
### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Era | Event |
|---|---|
| 1958 | Soviet Vorona-1 satellite (Earth-Moon L2) encounters hostile non-terrestrials; cosmonaut investigation team killed; incident buried |
| 1963 | Kennedy survives assassination (alternate history); Kletka Program announced: joint US-Soviet containment station ("Kletka": Russian for cage) built around the captured Typhon structure |
| 1980 | Pobeg Incident: Typhon kill scientists; Project Axiom shut down, Typhon left alive in containment |
| 2025 | TranStar Corporation acquires the Kletka hulk |
| 2030 | Refit complete; renamed Talos I; Neuromod program launches |
| 2032 | Station-wide outbreak during shareholder demonstration. **Edition pin** |

**Station orbit:** Earth-Moon L2 Lagrange point. **Sector codes:** LOB000 lobby, LOB01 shuttle bay, LOB02 neuromod division, LOB03 psychotronics (built around the original Kletka containment unit), LOB04 hardware labs, LIF000/001 life support and cargo **[canon]**.

### 1.3 Assessment of Forces

**TranStar Industries.** Earth-side corporate parent: quarterly-report ethics, unlimited research ambition.

**Station Security.** Operators with non-lethal disruptors by policy (liability), lethal options in armories by precedent.

**The Typhon.** See Part Six. Black shadow-matter organisms that read as "living physics experiments": they obey rules we have only begun to chart.

**Looking Glass Group / January Protocol.** A hidden contingency: recordings and a duplicate AI guiding selected personnel through outbreak response. Its existence implies leadership expected this **[W]**.

---

## PART TWO: TALOS I STATION

### 2.0 Structure

| Section | Function |
|---|---|
| Lobby / Arboretum | Public face; executive offices; the garden dome |
| Psychotronics | Typhon research wing: specimen containment, neuromod fabrication |
| Hardware Labs | Engineering workshops, prototype weapons |
| Life Support | Atmosphere processing; the station's lungs |
| Cargo Bay | Supply transfer; shuttle servicing |
| Crew Quarters | Residences, recreation (the Golden Guns pool hall), chapel |
| Shuttle Bay | Earth transit; quarantine screening |
| Power Plant | Reactor stack; self-destruct coupling |
| Deep Storage & Data Vaults | Archive levels; secrets live here |

```
   TALOS I — SPINE PROFILE (schematic)

   [SHUTTLE BAY]——[LOBBY]——[ARBORETUM]
        |             |           |
   [CREW QUARTERS]—[HARDWARE LABS]—[PSYCHOTRONICS]
        |             |           |
   [LIFE SUPPORT]—[CARGO]——[POWER PLANT]——[DEEP STORAGE]
```

### 2.01 Station Systems

- **Look-glass mirrors & GLOO cannon:** repair foam doubles as climbing medium and emergency barricade.
- **Operators:** robotic service units (medical, engineering, repair). Post-outbreak, hostile reprogramming suspected.
- **Recycler charges:** matter-to-material conversion grenades. Everything is inventory.
- **Psychoscope:** Typhon-detection helmet overlay revealing invisible organisms and weak points.

---

## PART THREE: TRANSPORT AND COMMUNICATIONS

Shuttle transfers to Earth run on schedule until quarantines. Internal transit: tram spine connecting sections plus maintenance crawlspaces. Communications: station intercom, personal transcribes, and Earth uplink via corporate relay. During the outbreak, every channel carries either propaganda, panic, or both.

---

## PART FOUR: RESEARCH ASSETS

### 4.1 Neuromods

**In brief:** Injectable skill packages: each neuromod wipes the user's recent memory (a documented side effect management prefers understated) and installs complete expertise: hacking, leverage, mimicking matter, firing weapons you never trained on.

- Fabricated from Typhon biomass at Psychotronics.
- Side effects include Typhon empathy drift at high usage counts **[W]**.

### 4.2 Prototype Weapons

| Item | Origin | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Disruptor Stun Gun | Security | Non-lethal; effective vs Operators and some Typhon |
| GLOO Cannon | Repair foam tech | Creates climbable foam; immobilizes small targets |
| Shotgun/Pistol prototypes | Hardware Labs | Conventional; ammo scarce |
| Recycler Charges | Salvage ops | Sucks in debris, detonates as shrapnel |

---

## PART FIVE: THREAT DOSSIERS

### 5.0 The Typhon

**In brief:** Shadow-black organisms of alien origin, apparently older than human civilization, operating as a coordinated ecology rather than individual monsters. They learn from us: every countermeasure we develop, their next generation has already seen.

| Organism | Behavior |
|---|---|
| **Mimic** | Disguises as everyday objects; swarms prey when approached. The station's signature horror |
| **Phantom** | Human-derived tall figures; elemental variants (thermal/electrical/pure); retain fragments of the person copied |
| **Technopath** | Hijacks Operators, turrets, and systems remotely |
| **Telepath** | Mind-control broadcast; compels humans to attack; vulnerable in dialogue before violence |
| **Nightmare** | Station-hunting apex form deployed against high-Typhon-aptitude personnel |

**Kill doctrine:** conventional fire works but wastes resources; Typhon die to disruption (Disruptor Stun Gun), fire, and especially to *their own* recycled neuromod material turned into weapons **[E]**.

### 5.1 Indoctrination Analogue: The Coral

**What:** Post-outbreak, Typhon growth ("the Coral") networks across the station exterior like neural tissue wrapped around the hull. It reads as a distributed brain forming around the station's bones.
**Cost of contact:** Touching it connects minds. Some connections do not fully close.

---

## PART SIX: COMMAND ORGANIZATION

```
TRANSTAR BOARD / CEO WILLIAM YU
        |
STATION EXECUTIVE: ALEX YU (Director of Research)
        |
   +------+----------+-------------+
   |      |          |             |
SECURITY  SCIENCE   SUPPORT        ENGINEERING
(Kaspar    (Psychotronics: (services,     (station systems,
 operators)  Dr. Kelstrup)  crew welfare)  power, trams)
   |
ETHICS OVERSIGHT — nominal; quarterly reports filed under marketing
```

- **Dual Yu dynasties:** Alex runs the station; brother Morgan is its most-modded test subject and conscience variable.
- **AI protocols:** January (and December) are recorded-message constructs guiding post-outbreak response. Their existence is the board's quiet admission of probability.

---

## PART SEVEN: HAZARD BESTIARY

### 7.0 Mimic Ambush Doctrine

**What:** The signature kill: an object you passed ten times was a Mimic for the last three.
**Doctrine:** Psychoscope sweeps, chair-counting discipline, and paranoia as a professional skill.

### 7.2 The Coral Cascade

**What:** Networked Typhon growth linking station systems to alien processing. Destroying the Coral threatens the station's structural integrity; preserving it feeds something larger.
**Cost of contact:** Choices about the Coral are choices about what humanity becomes next door to Earth.

### 7.3 Self-Destruct Contingency

**What:** Reactor overload scuttles Talos I into lunar surface rather than risk Earth contamination. Authorization: CEO biometric + reactor override.
**Cost of contact:** One lever ends the patent portfolio and the plague together.
---

## PART EIGHT: APPENDICES

### Appendix A: Glossary

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Coral | Typhon growth network enveloping the station |
| Mimic | Shapeshifting Typhon organism |
| Neuromod | Injectable skill/knowledge package |
| Operator | Robotic service unit |
| Phantom | Human-derived Typhon form |
| Psychoscope | Typhon-detection helmet system |
| Typhon | The alien organisms under study; station-wide threat |
| January/December | Contingency AI protocols (recorded guidance constructs) |

### Appendix B: Station Transit

| Route | Method |
|---|---|
| Section ↔ section | Tram spine + GLOO-climbed maintenance routes |
| Talos I ↔ Earth | Shuttle bay (quarantine locked at pin) |
| Exterior EVA | Airlocks + GLOO footholds; hull is walkable |

### Appendix C: Discrepancy Log and Writer's Latitude

| Topic | Variance across playthroughs | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| Morgan's choices | Player-variable (neuromod use, survivor outcomes) | Pin reflects outbreak day only; endings unresolved **[W]** |
| Typhon origin depth | Deep Storage reveals partial | Partial by ruling; the structure predates explanation |
| January vs December guidance | Contradictory protocols | Both are canon; choosing is the story |
| Casualty counts | Unstated | "Most of five hundred" stands |

**Writer's latitude principles:** companion-consistent. TranStar's invoices arrive as memory gaps, missing colleagues, and skills that came with someone else's dreams.

---

*End of manual. Thank you for your contribution to human progress.*

| DUNE - SPACING GUILD | Reign of Shaddam IV |


<!-- ====== VOLUME: DUNE - SPACING GUILD ====== -->

# VOLUME - DUNE - SPACING GUILD

*Reign of Shaddam IV*

---

# THE SPACING GUILD
## NAVIGATOR'S OPERATING HANDBOOK

*Eighteenth entry in the collected set; house-style variant: authored as Guild internal doctrine. The Guild sells certainty; this manual explains what it actually buys.*

---

## ABOUT THIS HANDBOOK

This handbook instructs Guild Navigators and steersmen in the conduct of foldspace travel, spice discipline, and the political neutrality that keeps the Imperium moving. It supplements judgment. The Spacing Guild holds a monopoly on interstellar transport because its Navigators are the only beings who can fold space safely, and this monopoly is the single most important fact of Imperial politics.

**Edition pin.** Current as of **the reign of Emperor Shaddam IV, prior to the Arrakis upheaval (Atreides accession)**.

---

## PART ONE: THE IMPERIAL THEATER

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Power | Domain | Relationship to the Guild |
|---|---|---|
| Padishah Emperor | Imperial throne, Sardaukar terror troops | Customer and hostage-holder |
| Landsraad Great Houses | Fiefdoms and private armies | Customers bound by Kanly (vendetta law) |
| CHOAM | Economic combine | Profit partner |
| Spacing Guild | All interstellar transport | Monopoly; politically neutral by charter |
| Bene Gesserit Sisterhood | Breeding program, Truthsayers | Uncomfortable equals |

Three facts define the Imperium:

1. **Who controls the spice controls the universe.** Melange extends life, unlocks prescience, and is the only substance that creates Navigators. It exists on one planet.
2. **Travel is monopoly, communication is not.** Guild ships move everything; messages ride Guild couriers too.
3. **Neutrality is enforced by dependence.** Every Great House needs the Guild more than the Guild needs any one house.

### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Era | Event |
|---|---|
| ~11,000+ years before pin | Butlerian Jihad: humanity bans thinking machines ("Thou shalt not make a machine in the likeness of a human mind"). Human mental training schools rise to replace computation |
| Founding era | Spacing Guild founded; first melange-mutated Navigators achieve safe foldspace travel; Guild receives monopoly in the Great Convention |
| Age of Padishah Emperors | Imperium stabilizes: Guild neutrality + CHOAM economics + Landsraad balance + Sardaukar fear |
| Pin era | Shaddam IV reigns; House Atreides accepts Arrakis fief; the cogs begin turning **[W]** |

---

## PART TWO: HEIGHLINER OPERATIONS

### 2.0 The Heighliner

**In brief:** A Guild heighliner is a city-sized starship carrying hundreds of smaller vessels between stars by folding space. Canon does not fix its length precisely; descriptions place them at many kilometers, holding fleets internally like a harbor.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Class | Foldspace cargo/passenger carrier |
| Scale | Many km class **[E]**; exact figures are Guild secrets |
| Complement | Thousands of crew, passenger berths for houses' retinues |
| Drive | Holtzman effect field generated by Navigator: space folds, ship does not "move" so much as exist elsewhere |

```
   HEIGHLINER — PROFILE (schematic)

   [NAVIGATOR TANK: forward command blister]
   __________/======================\__________
   ||  INTERNAL HARBOUR: frigates,            ||
   ||  liners, house warships at anchor       ||
   ||=========================================||
    \___ drive blisters ___/  \___ ... ___/
```

### 2.01 The Foldspace Jump

1. Navigator ascends the tank chamber and immerses in concentrated melange gas.
2. Prescience awakens: the Navigator *sees* the safe path through folded space.
3. Space folds; the heighliner and everything docked within arrives at destination.
4. Misfold without a Navigator: historically catastrophic. No exceptions to Navigator requirement.

**[W]** Foldspace travel has no transit time worth narrating. The drama is in the Navigator's vision: they see futures, including their own death paths, and steer around them.
---

## PART FOUR: IMPERIAL CRAFT AND GROUND OPERATIONS

| Craft | Operator | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Ornithopter ("thopter")** | All houses | Flittering-wing aircraft; Holtzman field lift; the workhorse of Arrakis skies |
| **Carryall** | Spice crews | Heavy-lift thopter ferrying harvesters |
| **Spice harvester** | Harkonnen/Atreides crews | Sandworm bait by definition; works in pairs with carryalls |
| **Frigates & House warships** | Great Houses | Carried as cargo by Guild heighliners; war arrives folded |
| **Sardaukar troop transports** | Emperor | Imperial terror delivery |
| **Sandcrawler equivalents** | Fremen (stillsuit-clad) | Fremen ride worms instead: Guild notes this with professional envy |

---

## PART FIVE: THE SPICE ECONOMY

**Melange:** geriatric spice, cinnamon-scented, blue-within-blue eye stain with chronic use. Effects:

1. Extends human lifespan by decades.
2. Unlocks prescient awareness in trained minds (Navigators, and some Sisters).
3. Is fatally addictive: withdrawal is death.

**Supply:** melange exists only on Arrakis, produced by sandworm lifecycle events nobody has replicated off-world.

**Guild doctrine:** the spice must flow. Guild prescience monitors all futures for threats to supply; the Guild's political interventions across ten millennia are all, ultimately, logistics.

**[W]** The Guild's neutrality ends exactly where spice supply is threatened. Everything else is negotiable, including emperors.

---

## PART SIX: THREAT DOSSIERS

### 6.1 Sandworms of Arrakis

**In brief:** Shai-Hulud: kilometer-scale sandworms guarding melange deposits. Vibrations attract them; shields drive them to frenzy (shield/lasgun interaction detonates like atomics).

**Engagement rules:** Do not walk with rhythm. Carry thumper decoys. Ride them the Fremen way only after you understand what that costs.

### 6.2 Bene Gesserit Sisterhood

**In brief:** The breeding-program Sisterhood: Truthsayers who read lies, prana-bindu body control, the Voice that commands obedience. They planted religions for their own contingencies (the Missionaria Protectiva) and are two generations from their own prescient goal (the Kwisatz Haderach).
**Relations:** Equal rivals. The Guild respects nothing else in the Imperium more.

### 6.3 The Harkonnen Question

House Harkonnen holds Arrakis at pin date under fief: brutality as extraction policy, gladiatorial cruelty as management style, and a feud with Atreides sanctioned as Kanly under the Great Convention. Their fall and return bracket the pin era **[W]**.

### 6.4 The Fremen

**In brief:** Deep-desert people adapted beyond Outsider comprehension: stillsuits recycling body water, worm-riding as transport, and a messianic expectation (Lisan al-Gaib) the Missionaria Protectiva accidentally seeded centuries ago.
**Assessment:** underestimated by everyone except the desert. That will change.

---

## PART SEVEN: GUILD COMMAND ORGANIZATION

```
GREAT CONVENTION (Imperial law: Kanly, atomics bans, Guild monopoly)
        |
SPACING GUILD HIGH COMMAND
        |
NAVIGATOR STEERING COMMITTEE   — mutated Navigators' council
        |
   +------+------------+----------------+
   |      |            |                |
HEIGHLINER  STEERSMAN    GUILD BANKS       PRESCIENCE
 FLEETS     ACADEMY     (financial arm)   AUDIT OFFICE
                        (CHOAM seats)
```

- **Navigator primacy:** mutated Navigators outrank unmutated Guild officers; humanity is optional at the top of the transport industry.
- **Neutrality enforcement:** houses violating Guild neutrality lose travel rights, which is economic death.
- **Prescience audit:** the Guild watches futures for spice-supply threats; it also watches other prescients watching back. Prescient blindness around certain bloodlines is on file and unsettling.

---

## PART EIGHT: BESTIARY OF THE IMPERIUM

### 8.0 Holtzman Effect

**What:** The physics substrate of the age: suspensors (gravity negation), shields (slow-penetrating defense), foldspace fields.
**Cost of contact:** Shields plus lasguns detonate like atomics. Every soldier knows; every battlefield fears the madman who tests it.

### 8.2 The Voice

**What:** Bene Gesserit direct-command modulation compelling obedience in nervous systems.
**Cost of contact:** Your own body obeys someone else. Training resists; innocence does not.

### 8.3 Kanly

**What:** Formal vendetta law governing Great House feuds: declared, bounded, ritualized war.
**Cost of contact:** Kanly makes feuds survivable. Breaking Kanly makes you the next common enemy.

### 8.4 Prescience Blindness

**What:** Certain bloodlines and substances hide their futures from Navigators and Seers alike.
**Where:** On file since Muad'Dib's line emerged **[post-pin W]**.
**Cost of contact:** For an institution built on seeing, blindness is both terror and opportunity.
---

## APPENDICES

### Appendix A: Glossary

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| CHOAM | Combine Honnete Ober Advancer Mercantiles; universal trade combine |
| Fremen | Deep-desert people of Arrakis |
| Heighliner | Guild foldspace carrier |
| Holtzman effect | Field physics underlying shields/suspensors/foldspace |
| Kanly | Formal vendetta under the Great Convention |
| Kwisatz Haderach | Bene Gesserit prescient superbeing target |
| Landsraad | Assembly of Great Houses |
| Melange / spice | Geriatric prescient drug; Arrakis-only |
| Sardaukar | Emperor's terror troops |
| Stillsuit | Body water-recycling suit |
| Voice | Bene Gesserit compulsion technique |

### Appendix B: Travel and Economics

| Route/Transaction | Notes |
|---|---|
| Interstellar transit | Guild heighliner only; Navigator required; no exceptions since founding |
| Spice contracts | Imperial quota system; Harkonnen-era production records inflated **[W]** |
| House troop transport | Warships ride as cargo; arrival is the political act |

### Appendix C: Discrepancy Log and Writer's Latitude

| Topic | Variance across sources (novels/films) | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| Heighliner dimensions | Never fixed on screen/page | "Many km" [E] stands; Guild secrecy is in-universe cover too |
| Prequels (Butlerian Jihad details) | Later-novel expansions | Jihad event canon; specifics beyond this volume's scope |
| Prescience mechanics | Evolves across sequels | Pin era: Navigator navigation + limited Seer glimpses |
| Worm sizes | "Bigger than any recorded" recurs | There is always a bigger worm. This is canon behavior, not hyperbole |

**Writer's latitude principles:** Dune's miracles are ecology and economics wearing robes. Every power has a supply chain, and the desert audits everyone.

---

*End of handbook. The spice must flow.*
