| DOCTOR WHO - TARDIS | Post-Time War era |


<!-- ====== VOLUME: DOCTOR WHO - TARDIS ====== -->

# VOLUME - DOCTOR WHO - TARDIS

*Post-Time War era*

---

# THE TARDIS TECHNICAL MANUAL

**TYPE 40, MARK III TT CAPSULE, TIME LORD ACADEMY PRESS**

*Nineteenth entry in the collected set; house-style variant: authored as a Time Lord Academy maintenance text, annotated over centuries by its most famous delinquent owner.*

---

## ABOUT THIS MANUAL

This manual documents the Type 40 time and relative dimension in space capsule: a living time machine older than most civilizations, currently masquerading as a 1963 Metropolitan police telephone box somewhere in the universe.

**Edition pin.** This manual covers the capsule in its **post-Time War, current-regeneration era**, owner: the Doctor. Ownership disputes are settled by the fact that she stole him first **[W]**.

**Conventions:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Verified Time Lord engineering |
| **[E]** | Extrapolated; the Doctor refuses to publish schematics |
| **[W]** | Writer's latitude note |

---

## PART ONE — DIMENSIONAL TRANSCENDENTALISM

### 1.0 Introduction

The TARDIS is bigger on the inside. Not "appears bigger": the interior exists in a separate dimensional plane tethered to the exterior shell. Interior volume has been mapped at effectively infinite; occupants have gotten lost for decades within its cloisters and swimming pools (the pool is near the wardrobe; this is not an excuse for either).

**In brief:** The shell is a door. Everything else is another universe with a doorbell.

### 1.1 Core Systems

| System | Function | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Chameleon Circuit | Shell disguises per environment | **Broken since 1963**: stuck as police box. Every repair attempt produces a police box |
| Time Rotor | Central column oscillating during flight | Currently glass panelling; groans audibly in flight (the sound IS the system) |
| Heart of the TARDIS | Living sentience powering all functions | Sentient. Opinionated. Chose the Doctor as much as he chose her **[W]** |
| Translation Field | Telepathic language matrix for crew/companions | Always on; makes every alien tongue English |
| Perception Filter | Makes the box "unnoticed" in plain sight | Functional when the plot allows it **[W]** |
| HADS (Hostile Action Displacement System) | Auto-dematerializes under attack | Frequently disabled by owners who prefer drama |
| Cloister Bell | Warning signal for imminent catastrophe | Sounds only when the universe itself is threatened |
| Temporal Grace | No physical violence possible inside the capsule while operational | Time Lord engineering; fails when the TARDIS is dying |
| Eye of Harmony | Exploded black hole (star) providing infinite power | Located beneath the console room; opening without authorization is a capital crime on Gallifrey |
| Zero Room | Dampens all sensory input for recovery from psychic trauma | Exists "inside" the TARDIS interior; accessible only when needed |
| Swimming Pool / Library / Wardrobe | Interior rooms exceeding exterior shell volume by orders of magnitude | Pool is near the wardrobe; this is not an excuse for either |
| Chameleon Arch | Biologically rewrites the owner into a human; stores Time Lord essence in a fob watch | Emergency identity concealment; memories return when watch is opened |
| Translation Matrix | Auto-translates all written/spoken languages for crew and companions | Linked to the TARDIS telepathic field, not the companion's own ability |

### 1.2 Flight Doctrine

Dematerialization requires the Time Rotor engaged and coordinates set on the six-sided console. The console room has cycled through several designs (white gleaming chrome to gothic coral to steampunk glass); the ship redecorates according to her moods between regenerations.

- **Flight is normally silent-instantaneous** from outside perspective.
- **The groaning wheeze** of materialization is a fault nobody will fix because everyone recognizes it.
- **Doors open on impact tolerance:** the wooden door has survived bullets, Dalek fire, and the vacuum of space. It is not just a door **[W]**.
---

## PART TWO — TIME TRAVEL DOCTRINE

### 3.1 The Laws of Time (as practiced)

1. **Non-intervention in fixed events.** Fixed points in time are load-bearing for history. Attempting to alter them causes catastrophe or is itself the event.
2. **No crossing your own timeline** without Blinovitch Limitation precautions: contact between a person's younger/older selves triggers energy discharge and worse.
3. **Paradoxes attract Reapers**: temporal entities that consume paradox sources, erasing the area from history.
4. **The Time War changed everything.** Pre-War Time Lord law assumed an institution enforcing it. Post-War, the last Time Lord enforces it by mood.

### 3.2 Temporal Phenomena

| Phenomenon | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Blinovitch Limitation Effect** | Energy discharge on self-timeline contact |
| **Time fissures/cracks** | Windows across time; things fall through both ways |
| **Fixed points** | Events history refuses to change (Rome burns; Capone falls) |
| **Reapers** | Paradox predators: erase contaminated zones from causality |
| **Bootstrap loops** | Information with no origin; stable but philosophically radioactive |

---

## PART THREE — THREAT DOSSIERS

### 3.0 Daleks

**In brief:** Mutant Kaled cores in armored travel machines: engineered hatred with tank treads and a plunger. Created by Davros on Skaro; survived their own creators' war repeatedly.

- **Doctrine:** EXTERMINATE as foreign policy. Time travel capability acquired during the Time War.
- **Weakness:** suction cup arm not rated for stairs (historically); belief systems immune to reason entirely.

### 3.1 Cybermen

**In brief:** Parallel-evolution humanity: Mondasian and Cybus variants converting organics into emotionless metal soldiers via "upgrading."

**Engagement rules:** Gold dust/nitro-9 disrupt respiratory systems; emotional suppressors fail when feelings leak back through. Cybermen win by patience and numbers.

### 3.2 The Master

**In brief:** The Doctor's oldest friend and mirror: another Time Lord, renegade, resurrected repeatedly, sometimes end-of-universe ambitious.

| Regeneration era | Modus operandi |
|---|---|
| Early incarnations | Diplomatic infiltration; UNIT sabotage; Auton alliance |
| Traken revival | Stole the Matrix; attempted Dalek alliance |
| End of Time | Immortality Gate; Master Race (every human converted to Master copies) |
| Missy | Cyberman army from dead humans; Nethersphere afterlife database; Time Lord Matrix infiltration |

**Status:** Always alive somewhere. That is the point of him **[W]**.

### 3.3 Weeping Angels

**In brief:** Quantum-locked predators: they exist only while unobserved, moving faster than sight when unseen. Images of an angel become angels. The only defense: don't blink.

### 3.4 The Time War

**What:** The Dalek-Time Lord conflict that burned the universe's history books. Billions of years of warfare compressed into one terrible day by temporal weapons. Ended when the Doctor used the Moment to destroy both sides simultaneously (Dalek fleet and Time Lord civilization together at the close of the last day of the War). Gallifrey was later revealed saved but displaced into a pocket universe.

**Cost of contact:** Survivors' guilt with a galaxy attached. The Doctor's refusal to use his name after the War is a wound, not a fashion choice.

---

## PART FOUR - TIME LORD PHYSIOLOGY AND SOCIETY

### 5.0 Biology

Two hearts (backup circulatory system), respiratory bypass (survive strangulation and vacuum exposure briefly), regeneration on terminal death (cellular rewrite into new body/personality; memories intact; personality shifts within core identity), telepathic contact with other Time Lords and their TARDIS, body temperature runs cold (60°F / 15°C baseline).

### 5.1 Gallifreyan Institutions

| Institution | Function |
|---|---|
| Lord President | Head of state; wears the Coronation Sash of Rassilon |
| High Council | Cardinal-level governance |
| Castellan | Security chief; Chancellery Guard |
| The Matrix | Supercomputer containing extracted minds of dead Time Lords; predictive and archival; can be entered physically via headplug |
| Academies | Prydonius (politics/the Doctor), Arcalia (science), Patrex (art) |

---

## PART FIVE - THE DOCTOR

**In brief:** Last (mostly) of the Time Lords, thief of a faulty TARDIS, wanderer since the Time War ended his people. Regenerates instead of dying: twelve-plus faces, one continuous memory, increasingly poor at goodbyes.

- **Companions:** the TARDIS travels best with humans aboard; they keep him kind.
- **The sonic screwdriver:** utility tool, magic wand by reputation; does not open deadbolts (wood). It is a confidence trick with a power supply, and it works.
- **Rules:** the Doctor lies about following rules, then follows them anyway at terrible cost.

**[W]** The Doctor is the format's exception that proves every rule in this set: a technical manual about a character who defeats manuals.

---

## PART SIX - GALLIFREYAN INSTITUTIONS AND COMMAND

```
LORD PRESIDENT OF GALLIFREY
        |
TIME LORD HIGH COUNCIL
        |
CELESTIAL INTERVENTION AGENCY   — the rules-enforcers who broke rules
        |
TIME LORD ACADEMY   — houses: Prydonius (the Doctor's), Arcalia, etc.
```

Gallifrey's fate: destroyed in the Time War, saved and hidden in a pocket universe at its end, currently misplaced again **[W]**. The Academy that issued this manual may exist outside causality now. Return postage uncertain.

---

## PART SEVEN - BESTIARY OF TIME AND SPACE

### 7.0 Fixed Points

**What:** Events woven into history so tightly that changing them unravels time itself.
**Cost of contact:** Even the Doctor obeys this one. Especially the Doctor.

### 7.1 The Time War

**What:** The Dalek-Time Lord conflict that burned the universe's history books: billions of years of warfare compressed into one terrible day, ending with the Moment and a choice the Doctor has regenerated around.
**Cost of contact:** Survivors' guilt with a galaxy attached.

### 7.2 Regeneration

**What:** Time Lord death-cheating: cellular renewal into a new body/personality, memories intact. Limited cycles historically (twelve); circumstances have expanded the budget **[W]**.
**Cost of contact:** You survive. Someone else wakes up wearing your life.

### 7.3 The Silence

**What:** Religious order whose members are forgotten the instant they leave your sight. Counting marks on skin recommended.
**Where:** Behind you, historically speaking.

---

## APPENDICES

### Appendix A: Glossary

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Blinovitch Limitation | Self-timeline contact physics |
| Chameleon Circuit | Disguise system (broken) |
| Gallifrey | Time Lord homeworld |
| Perception filter | Unnoticed-in-plain-sight field |
| Regeneration | Time Lord renewal |
| Sonic screwdriver | The Doctor's utility device |
| TARDIS | Time And Relative Dimension In Space |

### Appendix B: Travel Parameters

| Journey | Notes |
|---|---|
| Anywhere in space/time | Standard capability |
| "Wrong" destinations | Frequent; the ship chooses sometimes |
| Fixed points avoidance | Navigationally mandatory |

### Appendix C: Discrepancy Log and Writer's Latitude

| Topic | Variance across decades | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| TARDIS interior size | "Effectively infinite" | Infinite-ish stands |
| Regeneration limits | 12 → more via canon interventions | Expanded-cycles ruling stands **[W]** |
| Time War details | Revealed progressively | Progressive revelation IS canon |
| Chameleon circuit repair | Attempted, failed, occasionally faked | Still broken. It is a character choice |

**Writer's latitude principles:** Doctor Who stories succeed when the impossible has consequences. The TARDIS can go anywhere: which means every story must explain why here, why then, and why them.

---

*End of manual. The manual would like to note that its owner has annotated it in crayon.*

# VOLUME - STAR TREK - ENTERPRISE-E

*Federation - pin 2379*

---

# STAR TREK: U.S.S. ENTERPRISE NCC-1701-E TECHNICAL MANUAL

**SOVEREIGN-CLASS STARSHIP, FEDERATION STARFLEET**

*Twentieth entry in the collected set; house-style variant: written as a Starfleet Corps of Engineers orientation supplement. Dedicated respectfully alongside the Okuda/Sternbach originals on the same shelf.*

---

## ABOUT THIS MANUAL

This supplement documents the Sovereign-class starship *Enterprise* NCC-1701-E as configured following the Dominion War and the Shinzon incident (2379). It assumes familiarity with Federation starship fundamentals covered in the owner's existing technical literature, and extends them to this class.

**Edition pin.** Current as of **2379 CE**, following the *Enterprise*-E's return from Romulan space after the Reman coup attempt.

---

## PART ONE: THE POSTWAR FEDERATION

### 1.0 Introduction

The Dominion War ended in 2375 after two years of carnage that reshaped every fleet in the Alpha Quadrant. Starfleet spent 2375–2379 rebuilding, and the Sovereign class became the template: war-tested systems wrapped in an exploration hull, because Starfleet never stops being both.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Alpha/Beta Quadrants |
| Flagship status | *Enterprise*-E among Starfleet's most decorated active vessels |
| Key powers | Federation, Klingon Empire (ally), Romulan Star Empire (post-coup instability), Cardassian Union (rebuilding) |

### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline (Enterprise-E Service History)

| Date | Event |
|---|---|
| 2372 | Commissioned under Captain Jean-Luc Picard |
| 2373 | Earth invaded by Borg sphere; Enterprise-E follows cube back to 2063, ensures first contact with Vulcans |
| 2375 | Ba'ku relocation affair (Insurrection) |
| 2379 | Reman praetor Shinzon's thalaron-equipped flagship *Scimitar* destroyed in Bassen Rift; Lieutenant Commander Data lost **[W]** |
---

## PART TWO: SOVEREIGN-CLASS SYSTEMS

### 2.0 Ship Overview

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Class | Sovereign-class starship |
| Length | ~685 m |
| Decks | 24 |
| Complement | ~700–850 crew + mission specialists **[E]** |
| Cruising warp | Warp 8; maximum sustainable Warp 9.9+ **[E]** |
| Design doctrine | Post-Wolf 359 combat lessons integrated into an exploration hull; no saucer separation (battle bridge eliminated by design) |

```
   SOVEREIGN-CLASS — PROFILE (schematic)

   [DEFLECTOR/SENSOR ARRAY]
    \______ saucer-integrated hull ______
     |  main bridge (deck 1)             |
     |  ten-forward lounge · stellar     |
     |  cartography · science decks      |
     |____ engineering hull (M/ARA) _____|
          nacelles: twin pylons, dorsal
        [CAPTAIN'S YACHT: ventral dock, deck 7? **[E]**]
```

### 2.1 Systems by Deck (selected)

| Deck | Function |
|---|---|
| 1 | Main bridge, ready room, observation lounge |
| 2–4 | Senior staff quarters, conference facilities |
| 6 | Captain's yacht dock (Cousteau) |
| 8–11 | Science labs, stellar cartography, stellar sciences annex |
| 10 | Ten-Forward recreation lounge **[E]** |
| 13–15 | Main engineering (warp core), torpedo bays |
| 16+ | Shuttlebays, cargo, deuterium/antimatter storage |

### 2.2 Tactical Systems

- **Type-XII phaser arrays:** multiple emitter strips; post-Dominion War power routing upgrades.
- **Quantum torpedoes:** primary torpedo complement (forward/aft banks); photon torpedoes retained.
- **Regenerative shielding:** improved grid geometry over Galaxy-class; multi-layer harmonics.
- **Ablative hull elements** on select surfaces **[E]**.

### 2.3 Propulsion

Warp propulsion: class-leading warp core with improved plasma transfer conduits; the Sovereign platform held Warp 9.9+ sustainably after Dominion War refits **[E]**. Impulse: fusion reactors standard.

---

## PART THREE: FLEET OPERATIONS

Shuttle complements include Type-11 personnel shuttles and the captain's yacht *Cousteau* (ventral docking, emergency command vessel). Medical: full sickbay complex with EMH Mark I availability (the Doctor's bedside manner is a known software issue).

---

## PART FOUR: THREAT DOSSIERS

### 4.1 The Borg Collective

Post-2373 assessment: the collective remains the Federation's existential threat. Enterprise-E engagement history includes temporal intervention at First Contact. Current Borg strategic posture fragmented post-Unimatrix events **[W beyond scope]**.

### 4.2 Romulan Star Empire

Reman coup under Shinzon exposed Romulan political fragility; thalaron radiation weapons demonstrated genocide-scale capability aboard a single ship. The Bassen Rift battle validated cloak-defeating rift tactics. Post-2379 Romulan succession is unresolved **[E]**.

### 4.3 The Son'a / Ba'ku Affair

Insurrection-era operation: Federation-adjacent misconduct exposed and refused by this crew. Logged as precedent for orders-refusal ethics cases at the Academy.

---

## PART FIVE: COMMAND ORGANIZATION

Standard Starfleet: Command division (gold/red), Sciences (blue), Operations (yellow). Enterprise-E senior staff: captain (Picard), first officer Will Riker (departed for Titan post-2379), counselor Troi, chief engineer La Forge, Dr. Crusher (head of Starfleet Medical rotating), Worf (returned to Klingon service), Data (lost 2379; B-4 legacy program pending) **[W]**.

---

## PART SIX: BESTIARY

### 6.0 Temporal Incursion Doctrine

**What:** Borg temporal vortex technology demonstrated at Earth 2373.
**Doctrine:** pursue through vortices only with full crew consent; changing one's own past is the heaviest bill in this set.

### 6.1 Thalaron Radiation

**What:** Romulan-developed biogenic radiation: instant organic tissue destruction at planetary scale from a single device.
**Where:** Scimitar wreckage; proliferation watch active.
**Cost of contact:** A single emitter is a species-level threat.

### 6.2 The Briar Patch

**What:** Metreon-cloud region: sensor-hostile, metaphasic-radiation pockets, Son'a industrial presence.
**Cost of contact:** Ships that hide there find things older than the Federation.
---

**Key locations (deck orientation, TNG-manual convention):** Main bridge (deck 1), observation lounge adjacent; Ten-Forward recreation (deck 10 [E]); stellar cartography annex (deck 11); main engineering and warp core (decks 13-15); shuttlebays aft (16+); captain's yacht Cousteau ventral dock.
|---|---|
| Earth ↔ Romulus (Neutral Zone crossing) | Days |
| Earth ↔ Deep Space stations | Hours–days by warp factor |
| Sector patrol loops | Standard rotation weeks |

### Appendix C: Discrepancy Log and Writer's Latitude

| Topic | Variance | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| Sovereign length | 680–685 m across sources | ~685 m stands |
| Deck count | 24 vs. 29 sources | 24 pinned for -E |
| Crew complement | 700–850 range | Pinned range **[E]** |
| Data's B-4 resurrection | Film-ambiguous post-credits | Logged as unresolved legacy program **[W]** |
| Captain's yacht deck | Never shown docked on screen | [E] pin: ventral, deck 6–7 region |

**Writer's latitude principles:** companion-consistent. Starfleet pays its bills in logs, tribunals, and the occasional lost officer who deserved better.

---

*End of supplement. Live long and prosper.*

# VOLUME - PUSHING ICE

*Deep field - settlement era*

---

# PUSHING ICE — ROCKHOPPER EXPEDITION REFERENCE

**LIND SALVAGE & EXPLORATION CHARTER — FIRST EDITION**

*Twenty-first volume in the collected set; house-style variant: authored as an expedition operations log for a single-ship charter. Canon derives from one novel; areas the source never explored are marked honestly rather than filled.*

---

## ABOUT THIS MANUAL

This reference documents the *Rockhopper* expedition: a comet-mining crew who chased a departing moon out of the solar system and became founders of humanity's interstellar diaspora. It supplements character drama; the source's central story is a friendship that breaks a crew and a timeline that breaks every promise home.

**Edition pin.** This manual reflects status quo as of **the settlement era following arrival and first Fountainhead contact**: the expedition stranded aboard Janus at its destination, Earth 260 years in the past-lightcone.

**Conventions used throughout:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Established by the source novel |
| **[E]** | Extrapolation where the novel was silent |
| **[W]** | Writer's latitude note |

---

## PART ONE — THE DEEP-FIELD THEATER

### 1.0 Introduction

In 2057 the crew of the *Rockhopper* pushed ice: herding comets back to human space for water, fuel, and profit. Then Saturn's moon Janus shed its disguise, accelerated past every probe humanity owned, and left the solar system under its own power. The Rockhopper was the only ship close enough to follow. Nobody told them the door would lock behind them.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Janus interior/settlement; origin Sol System 260 light-years and 260 years distant |
| Complement | Original crew ~40 **[E]** plus generations born en route |
| Powers (expedition) | Lind loyalists vs Barseghian faction vs neutral skilled trades |
| External powers | Fountainheads (benevolent, secretive), Musk Dogs (hostile empire), absent Spican builders |

Three facts define everything:

1. **The trip is one-way and was known to be.** Earth falsified fuel telemetry so the crew would commit. Trust in distant authority died first and stayed dead.
2. **Time runs differently for everyone else.** Thirteen years aboard equals centuries outside; every era they reach has forgotten their departure.
3. **Leadership legitimacy fractured on day one** and never fully healed: two founders, two philosophies, one population.

### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Date | Event |
|---|---|
| 2057 CE | Rockhopper mining routine interrupted: Janus sheds ice camouflage, accelerates toward Spica (260 ly). Rockhopper intercepts; captured by Janus wake-field |
| Capture + days | Crew schism: land aboard Janus or drop out and die waiting for rescue. Captain Bella Lind prevails; chief engineer Svetlana Barseghian's faction defeated; friendship ruptures |
| Voyage (13 yrs subjective / ~260 yrs Earth) | Community built inside Janus; births, deaths, politics |
| Arrival | Automated machinery raises the "iron sky" over Janus surface; contact with the Fountainheads |
| Settlement era | Rejuvenation gifted; truth about the "zoo" withheld; Musk Dog threat emerges; Janus ultimately destroyed by Musk Dog assault **[post-pin W]** |

### 1.5 Status Quo Snapshot (Settlement Era)

| Domain | Status |
|---|---|
| Population | Expedition survivors + descendants; rejuvenation extends lifespans indefinitely **[E]** |
| Governance | Dual-founder tradition: Lind line and Barseghian line alternate/split authority **[W]** |
| Technology | Human baseline + Fountainhead gifts (rejuvenation); Spican automation maintains environment |
| Threats | Musk Dogs inbound historically; existential boredom and factionalism internally |
| Home | Sol system: 260 years unreachable, eventually 18 millennia |

---

## PART TWO — THE ROCKHOPPER

### 2.0 Vessel Overview

**In brief:** A nuclear-powered comet miner: industrial hull, spin habitats, ice-shepherding equipment, never designed for what it became. The most important spacecraft in human history by outcome, if not by specification.

| Property | Value **[E]** unless noted |
|---|---|
| Class | Comet-mining vessel ("ice-pusher") |
| Length | ~200 m class **[E]** |
| Complement | ~40 original crew **[E]**; settlement population grows thereafter |
| Drive | Nuclear fusion torch; high-thrust capable; captured-wake FTL via Janus field |
| Armament | Mining nuclear devices ("no one else gets our comet") and cutting gear |
| Systems | Ice-shepherd arrays, refinery stock, fabrication shops, hypersleep medical bay |

```
   ROCKHOPPER — PROFILE (schematic)

   [COMET-SHEPHERD ARRAYS / TUG RIG]
    \__ command module · spin habitat __/
     |  refinery · tankage · shops      |
     |__ mining nuke magazine __________|
         [FUSION TORCH ENGINES x3]
```

### 2.01 Systems by Station

| Station | Function |
|---|---|
| Command deck | Flight ops, company comms (dead network), navigation |
| Spin habitat | Quarters, mess, medbay; the social world of the crew |
| Refinery deck | Ice processing retrofitted to settlement industry |
| Engineering | Reactor plant; Svetlana Barseghian's domain and power base |

**[W]** The ship is a workplace that became a nation. Every system doubles as municipal infrastructure once you stop going home.
---

## PART THREE — CAPTURE AND TRANSIT OPERATIONS

### 3.0 The Wake-Field Capture

**In brief:** Janus projects a field that caught the Rockhopper during shadowing: acceleration matched, escape mathematically impossible. The crew's choice was never "go home or stay"; it was "land and live aboard, or drift dead in the black."

**Transit physics (observed):**

| Effect | Detail |
|---|---|
| Time dilation | 13 years ship-time ≈ 260 years Earth-frame at cruise |
| Wake capture | No fuel cost to the captured vessel; the field carries all |
| Course | Fixed: Spica approach corridor; no steering authority granted |

---

## PART FOUR — JANUS AND EXTERNAL CRAFT

### 4.1 Janus (Machine-Moon)

**In brief:** A 200-km-class **[E]** moon-shell that is an automated starship: ice camouflage over machine strata, interior cavities converted to habitat space for passengers it collected across deep time.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Class | Automated collector-vessel (Spican origin) |
| Automation | Full environmental maintenance; no crewed contact from builders ever |
| Defenses | Passive; ultimately destroyed by Musk Dog assault **[post-pin]** |
| Mission | Deliver gathered civilizations to a gathering structure |

**Interior doctrine:** the crew lands on the surface, lives under manufactured conditions, and never meets the owners. The ship is a bus with nobody driving.

### 4.2 Fountainhead Structures

Vast jellyfish-mountain organisms/structures at destination: benevolent, ancient, communicative on their own terms, masters of rejuvenation biotech. They share gifts sparingly and truths never fully.

### 4.3 Musk Dog Craft **[E]**

Hostile civilization's vessels: raiders who eventually overwhelm and destroy Janus. Their doctrine is domination of weaker gathered species.

---

## PART FIVE — EXPEDITION ARMAMENT AND EQUIPMENT

- **Mining nuclear devices:** one deployed to deny the abandoned comet to rivals; magazine retained as strategic reserve.
- **Cutting/boring gear:** industrial; doubles as construction equipment for settlement.
- **Vacuum-rated suits and heavy plant:** survival infrastructure.
- **Rejuvenation technology (Fountainhead gift):** biological age reset; transforms population demographics permanently.

**Strategic materials:** water-ice stockpiles, refined metals from refinery stores, and the irreplaceable human skill base: forty people's entire professional knowledge.

---

## PART SIX — THREAT DOSSIERS

### 6.1 The Schism (internal)

**In brief:** The defining threat was never alien. Bella Lind's decision to land split the crew; Svetlana Barseghian's faction considered it kidnapping. Two competent leaders ran parallel power structures for years, reconciled by circumstance, ruptured again by principle.

**Engagement rules:** Never resolve the Lind/Barseghian rift permanently; its recurrence is the setting's engine. Every crisis re-litigates the original landing decision.

### 6.2 The Musk Dogs

An expansionist carnivorous civilization encountered in the gathering structure: brutal by temperament and empire by habit. They destroy Janus in the historical record. Their arrival forces former enemies into coalition **[W]**.

### 6.3 Institutional Deception (the standing threat)

Earth falsified telemetry. Bella withheld Earth's catastrophes from the crew. Fountainheads concealed the zoo truth. The pattern repeats at every scale: *information is rationed for your own good.* Every generation rediscovers a lie its founders lived inside.

---

## PART SEVEN — COMMAND ORGANIZATION

```
CAPTAIN BELLA LIND              CHIEF ENGINEER SVETLANA BARSEGHIAN
(command legitimacy)            (technical legitimacy)
        \                          /
     SETTLEMENT COUNCIL (post-schism compromise)
        |
   +----+------+----------+
   |           |          |
LIFE SUPPORT  INDUSTRY   SCOUTS/MEDICAL
(plant, air)  (refinery)  (Maru-analog roles **[E]**)
```

- **Dual-founder doctrine:** neither founder could exile the other without destroying the colony's legitimacy. Successive generations institutionalized the balance.
- **Far-future legacy:** the Congress of the Lindblad Ring (18,000+ years later) honors Lind as "the Benefactor"; the expedition's choices became a civilization's founding myth **[W]**.

---

## PART EIGHT — BESTIARY OF THE DEEP FIELD

### 8.0 The Iron Sky

**What:** A roof of automated construction raised over Janus before arrival: sky as hatch cover.
**Where:** Janus surface, destination system (actual location: millions of light-years off, see §8.2).
**Cost of contact:** It converts "sky" into a lid. Claustrophobia becomes cosmology.

### 8.2 The Gathering Structure ("Zoo")

**What:** A megastructure containing multiple transplanted civilizations, assembled by the Spicans because intelligent life almost never coexists in time. The guests are separated by epochs and light-years; the zoo is where extinct timelines meet.
**Cost of contact:** Every neighbor is a ghost of someone else's era. Nothing here is contemporary with anything.

### 8.3 Femtotech Cube

**What:** A black cube of morphable femtotechnology sent as aid by far-future humanity inspired by the Benefactor legend: godlike capability delivered as a thank-you note across eighteen millennia.
**Where:** Late-era settlement records.
**Cost of contact:** Responsibility without instructions.

### 8.4 Relativistic Estrangement

**What:** The standing condition: every year aboard is decades outside; everyone the crew knew is dead before arrival; new arrivals (none come) would be younger than the colony's grandchildren.
**Cost of contact:** Homesickness with no home left in the present tense.

---

## APPENDICES

### Appendix A — Glossary

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Iron sky | Automated roof constructed over Janus at destination |
| Janus | Saturnian "moon"; automated Spican collector-vessel |
| Fountainheads | Massive benevolent aliens of the gathering structure |
| Musk Dogs | Hostile carnivore civilization |
| Pushing ice | Comet-mining profession |
| Rockhopper | The expedition vessel |
| Spicans | Absent builders of Janus and the accelerator |
| The Benefactor | Far-future honorific for Bella Lind |

### Appendix B — Transit and Time

| Event | Duration |
|---|---|
| Sol → Janus capture | Days of pursuit |
| Voyage to destination | 13 yrs ship / ~260 yrs Earth |
| Earth → Lindblad Ring era | ~18,000 years |

### Appendix C — Discrepancy Log and Writer's Latitude

| Topic | Variance | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| Rockhopper dimensions | Never stated numerically | ~200 m class **[E]** stands |
| Crew headcount | ~40 implied | [E] pin stands |
| Fountainhead motives | Deliberately unresolved | Remain unresolved **[W]** |
| Post-novel eras | Novel skips ahead | Skipped eras are writable history, not blank canon **[W]** |
| Sequel material | None published | Standalone universe ruling: no Revelation Space crossover |

**Writer's latitude principles:** this volume covers a single novel; vast areas are unexplored canon. Depth comes from consequence, not invention: extrapolate politics, generations, and consequences; never retrofit fake specifications into Reynolds' silences.

---

*End of manual. Filed from the settlement, under an iron sky, long after home stopped being a place.*

| BATTLETECH | Inner Sphere - pin 3025 Succession Wars |

# VOLUME - BATTLETECH

*Inner Sphere - pin 3025 Succession Wars*

---

# BATTLETECH — INNER SPHERE TECHNICAL READOUT

**COMSTAR SANCTIONED REPRINT — SUCCESSION WARS ERA**

*Twenty-second entry in the collected set; house-style variant: authored as a ComStar-approved technical summary of BattleMech forces circa 3025.*

---

## ABOUT THIS READOUT

This document catalogs BattleMech designs, transport doctrine, and Successor State forces of the Inner Sphere as of 3025. Star League-era specifications are reproduced where surviving copies permit; much has been lost.

**Edition pin.** Current as of **3025 CE, Fourth Succession War brewing**: five Great Houses, centuries of lostech, and a ComStar that controls every faster-than-light message in human space.

---

## PART ONE — THE INNER SPHERE

### 1.0 Introduction

Humanity spread across a thousand worlds under the Star League, then destroyed that golden age in one coup. Three Succession Wars later, the Five Great Houses fight with regressing technology over ruins, while ComStar quietly owns every message between the stars.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Inner Sphere, ~600 light-year radius around Terra |
| Powers | Five Successor States: Federated Suns (Davion), Draconis Combine (Kurita), Lyran Commonwealth (Steiner), Free Worlds League (Marik), Capellan Confederation (Liao) |
| Neutral power | ComStar: Keeper of the HPG network, based on Terra |
| Warfare standard | BattleMech duels governed by the Ares Conventions |

Defining facts:

1. **Lostech is real.** Each war destroys more Star League knowledge than universities can rebuild. A working Star League-era laser is a treasure.
2. **JumpShips are sacrosanct.** Attacking one invites total retaliation; all strategy flows through fixed jump points at stars' zenith and nadir.
3. **BattleMechs decide everything.** A single Mech can take or hold a world the old way: by standing on it.
---

## PART TWO — TRANSPORT CHAIN: JUMPSHIPS AND DROPSHIPS

### 2.0 JumpShip Operations

**In brief:** Interstellar travel belongs to JumpShips: fragile sail-equipped vessels that charge at a star's zenith or nadir jump point, then translate up to thirty light-years per jump. They never fight. K-F drive recharge takes roughly a week with a solar sail.

| Property | Value **[E]** |
|---|---|
| Jump range | 30 light-years per jump |
| Recharge | ~175 hours (sail) |
| Collar capacity | 4 DropShips typical |
| Doctrine | Never jump close to gravity wells; never fight |

**DropShips** carry the war: Union-class (12 Mechs + fighters) and Overlord-class (36 Mechs) assault birds deliver lances from jump point to planetfall in days.

### 2.1 Hyperpulse Generators (HPG)

ComStar's HPG stations beam instant messages between worlds on a paid schedule. ComStar owns the network; ComStar reads the mail; ComStar interdicts worlds that annoy it. Every Successor Lord tolerates this because nobody can replace it.

---

## PART FOUR-A — BATTLEMECH RECOGNITION (SELECTED READOUTS)

| Mech | Tonnage | Movement | Key armament | Role |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Atlas AS7-D** | 100 t | 3/5 walk/run | AC/20, LRM-20, SRM-6, 4 Medium Lasers | Assault terror weapon; its cockpit art alone breaks militia morale |
| **Marauder MAD-3R** | 75 t | 4/6 | 2 PPCs, AC/5, 2 Small Lasers | Fire-support line holder |
| **Warhammer WHM-6R** | 70 t | 4/6 | 2 PPCs, 2 Machine Guns, SRM-6 | Direct-fire brawler |
| **Archer ARC-2R** | 70 t | 4/6 | 2 × LRM-20 racks | Missile bombardment |
| **Rifleman RFL-3N** | 60 t | 4/6 | 2 AC/5s, 2 Large Lasers | Anti-air and anti-light |
| **Catapult CPLT-C1** | 65 t | 4/6 | 2 × LRM-15, 4 Medium Lasers | Fire support |
| **Phoenix Hawk PXH-1** | 45 t | 6/9, Jump 6 | Medium Laser, Large Laser, MGs | Cavalry scout |
| **Locust LCT-1V** | 20 t | 8/12 | 3 Medium Lasers | Fastest recon skirmisher |

Heights run eight to fourteen meters by class; a hundred-ton Atlas stands over thirteen.

**Doctrine:** lances of four fight in weight classes; ammunition dependence shapes campaigns (autocannon and missile resupply is logistics); heat management decides duels (every weapon fires against a heat budget).
---

## PART FIVE — WEAPONS AND MATERIALS

| Weapon class | Examples | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Autocannons (AC/2–AC/20) | Chemically-fired shells | Ammo-dependent; AC/20 hits like a building falling |
| PPCs | Particle Projection Cannons | No ammo; heat-hungry; the nobleman's gun |
| Lasers | Small/Medium/Large | Lostech frontier: Star League pulse and ER variants gone |
| LRMs/SRMs | Missile racks | Salvo saturation; ammo explosions gut internal structure |
| Machine guns | Anti-infantry | Cheap, brutal, everywhere |

**Lostech register:** Endo-Steel skeletons, Ferro-Fibrous armor, Double Heat Sinks, ER and Pulse variants, CASE ammunition shielding. Every entry is Star League inheritance the Successor States can no longer manufacture reliably.

---

## PART SIX — THREAT DOSSIERS

### 6.1 The Five Successor Houses

| House | State | Character |
|---|---|---|
| Davion | Federated Suns | Industrial might; combined-arms doctrine |
| Kurita | Draconis Combine | Samurai-coded honor culture; elite swordsmen regiments |
| Steiner | Lyran Commonwealth | Money and metal; socially liberal, militarily heavy |
| Marik | Free Worlds League | Fractious parliament; good warships |
| Liao | Capellan Confederation | Smallest, most paranoid; Warrior Houses as fanatical elites |

### 6.2 ComStar

The semi-religious order running Terra and every HPG. Publicly: neutral communicators. Actually: hoarding Star League caches, playing houses against each other, waiting for the day interstellar civilization needs its only messenger to become its only master **[W]**.

### 6.3 Mercenaries

Wolf's Dragoons and a thousand lesser commands sell lances by the contract. The Ares Conventions civilize the trade: no atrocity without consequence, ransoms honored, wars scheduled. Break the Conventions and every mercenary house declines your next contract.

---

## PART SEVEN — COMMAND ORGANIZATION

```
FIRST LORD (Star League throne: VACANT since the Coup)
        |
SUCCESSOR LORDS (5)     — each claims the mantle
        |
HOUSE MILITARY COMMANDS → Regimental combat teams
        |
COMSTAR (nominal neutral) — HPG monopoly + Terra garrison
MERCENARY GUILD          — contract arbitration
```

---

## PART EIGHT — BESTIARY OF THE SUCCESSION WARS

### 8.0 Lostech

**What:** The steady disappearance of capability: each Succession War burns libraries and shipyards.
**Cost of contact:** Your grandfather's mech was better than yours, and nobody alive knows why it worked.

### 8.2 JumpPoint Interdiction

**What:** Pirates ambush DropShip convoys arriving at recharge points.
**Cost of contact:** Every arrival is an unguarded moment; escorts exist for this reason.

### 8.3 ComStar Interdiction

**What:** HPG blackout as diplomatic weapon: worlds cut off from the galaxy until they comply.
**Cost of contact:** Weeks of silence that end governments.

---

## APPENDICES

### Appendix A — Glossary

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| BattleMech | 20–100 ton walking war machine |
| HPG | Hyperpulse Generator; FTL comms |
| K-F drive | Kearny-Fuchida jump drive |
| Lance | Four-Mech tactical unit |
| Lostech | Star League technology lost to war |
| Ares Conventions | Rules-of-war accord governing noble warfare |

### Appendix B — Travel Times

| Route | Method | Duration |
|---|---|---|
| Jump point → planet | DropShip burn | Days |
| 30 light-year hop | JumpShip K-F translation | Instant + ~7 day recharge |

### Appendix C — Discrepancy Log and Writer's Latitude

| Topic | Variance across sourcebooks | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| Mech heights | 8–14 m by tonnage, art varies | Class-based pins stand |
| Clan Invasion timing | Post-3049 | Beyond this edition's pin |
| ComStar intentions | Revealed progressively | Hidden by ruling until canon breaks them open |

**Writer's latitude principles:** the Inner Sphere runs on inherited glory and declining parts. Every battle spends history.

---

*End of readout. ComStar thanks you for your subscription.*

# VOLUME - REVELATION SPACE

*Yellowstone Expanse - Resurgem crisis 2526*

---

# REVELATION SPACE — LIGHTHUGGER OPERATIONS REFERENCE

**YELLOWSTONE EXPANSE SURVIVAL BULLETIN**

*Twenty-third entry in the collected set; house-style variant: authored as an Ultranaut survival bulletin. Hard-science voice throughout; Reynolds' universe punishes miracles.*

---

## ABOUT THIS BULLETIN

This reference documents the Yellowstone Expanse, Conjoiner drive travel, and the Inhibitor crisis as understood by lighthugger crews. It is written for people who already know the void is trying to kill them.

**Edition pin.** Current as of **the Resurgem crisis period (c. 2526 CE)**: the *Nostalgia for Infinity* arrived over Yellowstone, the Melding Plague has gutted Chasm City and the Glitter Band, and something old has woken in the dark between stars.

---

## PART ONE — THE YELLOWSTONE EXPANSE

### 1.0 Introduction

Humanity reached the stars in slow lighthuggers crewed by Ultranauts, settled dozens of systems, and built paradise around Yellowstone: the Glitter Band, thousands of orbitals running direct-democracy by neural implant. Then a passenger from Resurgem brought back more than memories, and the Plague ate paradise from inside. Now the sky belongs to ruins, and something in deep space has noticed us.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Yellowstone system (Epsilon Eridani), Glitter Band ruins, Chasm City surface |
| Polities | Remnant Demarchist authority, Panoply law enforcement **[E]**, Ultranaut fleet remnants, Shrouder territories |
| Existential threats | Melding Plague (active, mutating), Inhibitors (awakening) |

Defining facts:

1. **The Plague eats both flesh and machine**, corrupting nanotech into grotesque hybrid growths. Chasm City's towers are its sculpture garden.
2. **Conjoiner drives work; nobody knows why anymore.** The Conjoiners build them and stop answering questions.
3. **The Inhibitors have been awake for millions of years,** pruning starfaring species on schedule. Recent events tripped their threshold.
---

## PART TWO — LIGHTHUGGER OPERATIONS

### 2.0 The Lighthugger

**In brief:** Kilometer-scale Conjoiner-drive starships: black, windowless, faster than anything organic-built has a right to be. Crewed by Ultranauts modified to endure decades of acceleration and vacuum-adjacent life. The *Nostalgia for Infinity* is the type's haunted flagship.

| Property | Value **[E]** |
|---|---|
| Length | 1,500–2,000 m class |
| Hull | Black ablative sheathing; no windows by design (radiation and sanity both) |
| Drive | Conjoiner engine: runs on exotic physics, sealed by the Conjoiners themselves |
| Complement | Dozens of Ultranauts; cryogenic passenger berths for colonists |
| Transit | Interstellar crossings measured in years to decades |

**Ultranaut modifications:** pressure-tolerant physiology, neural interface grafts, pain management cultures. Ultras age slowly, heal oddly, and consider baseline humans fragile cousins.

```
   LIGHTHUGGER — PROFILE (schematic)

   BOW [torpedo/ram shielding]
    \____ crew spine (rotational drift sections) ____/
     |__ cargo · cryo berths · Conjoiner sanctum ___|
          [DRIVE CAGE: do not open. ever.]
```

### 2.1 The Conjoiner Drive Cage

Each lighthugger's drive is a sealed Conjoiner enclave: Exultants living inside the engine, thinking it into operation. Opening the cage kills the ship's soul and invites what the Conjoiners fled. This is not superstition; two ships proved it **[W]**.

---

## PART THREE — COMMUNICATIONS AND TIME

No FTL communication exists: messages ride ships. Yellowstone-to-Resurgem news arrives years stale. Consequence: every polity governs itself in isolation, every war ends before orders arrive, and every reunion happens between strangers.

---

## PART FOUR — VESSEL AND FACTION PROFILES

| Entity | Profile |
|---|---|
| *Nostalgia for Infinity* | Ancient lighthugger, plague-scarred, partially Inhibitor-corrupted during deep-space salvage; her Ultras are few and strange |
| Demarchist orbitals | Glitter Band ruins: polling-implant democracies devoured by Plague growths |
| Chasm City surface | Plague-mutated metropolis; the rich live in the sealed Canopy Ascent |
| Shrouder territory | Alien stealth civilization; contact returns crews changed or not at all |

---

## PART FIVE — WEAPONS AND MATERIALS

- **Cache weapons:** Hell-class superweapons recovered from buried caches; they have their own agendas and require negotiation with their internal intelligences before firing **[W]**.
- **Green beam:** Inhibitor signature weapon observed at Resurgem: precision orbital strike from interstellar range.
- **Warp-capable? No.** Nothing here cheats light except the drives, and the drives answer to Conjoiners.

---

## PART SIX — THREAT DOSSIERS

### 6.0 The Inhibitors

**In brief:** A machine network a billion years old, seeded to suppress starfaring intelligence until some ancient galactic crisis passes. They wake when civilizations cross a threshold, then sterilize them with patient, industrial horror: wolf-shaped hunters, neutron-star-forged machinery, and time on their side.

**Engagement rules:** You do not fight the Inhibitors. You hide, you run, or you become part of their evidence.

### 6.1 The Melding Plague

Nanotechnological plague that rewrites both machine and flesh into baroque hybrid growths. It cannot be cured, only survived: abandon infected tech, amputate infected tissue, and never trust a "cure." Chasm City is its masterpiece.

### 6.2 The Shrouders

Stealth-wrapped alien species whose territories rewrite physics as defense. Probing them bought humanity exactly one useful technology and a permanent warning label.
---

## APPENDICES

### Appendix A — Glossary

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Conjoiner drive | Sealed exotic-physics starship engine |
| Demarchist | Yellowstone direct-democracy polity |
| Inhibitor | Billion-year machine network pruning starflight |
| Lighthugger | Kilometer-scale Conjoiner-drive starship |
| Melding Plague | Nanotech plague corrupting tech and flesh |
| Pattern Jugglers | Ocean-memory alien organisms |
| Shrouders | Stealth-wrapped alien civilization |
| Ultranaut | Modified lighthugger crew |

### Appendix B — Transit Times

| Route | Duration |
|---|---|
| Yellowstone ↔ Resurgem system | Years **[E]** |
| Interstellar crossing (Conjoiner drive) | Decades at high fraction of c |
| Messages between systems | Ride ships; no FTL comm |

### Appendix C — Discrepancy Log and Writer's Latitude

| Topic | Variance across novels | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| Series chronology overlap | Novels interleave eras | Each story pins its own year; cross-reference carefully |
| Inhibitor motives | Revealed across trilogy | Progressive by design **[W]** |
| Plague behavior | Evolves per location | Local mutation doctrine stands |
| Conjoiner drive internals | Deliberately unexplained | Unexplained by ruling **[W]** |

**Writer's latitude principles:** Reynolds' universe enforces consequences at lightspeed and beyond. Nothing arrives on time; everything costs decades.

---

*End of bulletin. Avoid the dark shapes between the stars.*

# VOLUME - FOUNDATION

*Galactic Empire - pin 498 FE*

---

# FOUNDATION — GALACTIC EMPIRE REFERENCE

**TERMINUS ENCYCLOPAEDIA SUPPLEMENT — FIRST EDITION**

*Twenty-fourth entry in the collected set; house-style variant: authored as a Foundation-era Encyclopaedia Galactica supplement. Psychohistorical projections cited where applicable.*

---

## ABOUT THIS MANUAL

This reference documents the Galactic Empire at its twilight, the rise of the Foundation on Terminus, and the psychohistorical forces shaping humanity's next million years. It supplements character work; Asimov's drama is ideas wearing people as costumes, and every system here exists because Hari Seldon calculated that it must.

**Edition pin.** Current as of **498 FE**, during Golan Trevize's search for Earth following the revelation of Gaia (see §1.5).

**Conventions used throughout:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Established by published novels |
| **[E]** | Extrapolation |
| **[W]** | Writer's latitude note |

---

## PART ONE — THE GALACTIC THEATER

### 1.0 Introduction

The Galactic Empire spans twenty-five million worlds. It has stood for twelve thousand years. Hari Seldon proved mathematically that it will fall regardless of any individual's action, and built two Foundations to shorten the dark age that follows from thirty millennia to one thousand. The Plan is running. The Mule broke it. The Second Foundation repaired it. And now someone is asking whether it should be running at all.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Milky Way Galaxy, ~25 million inhabited worlds |
| Capital | Trantor (administrative metal world, 40 billion population); Foundation capital on Terminus at galaxy's edge |
| Polities | First Foundation (Terminus), Second Foundation (location hidden), Galactic Empire (Trantor, declining), Gaia (planetary super-organism) |

Three facts define strategy:

1. **Psychohistory works on crowds, not individuals.** Billions of minds produce predictable behavior; one person with the right power can break every projection.
2. **The Empire cannot be saved.** Seldon's equations prove decline is structural: bureaucratic inertia, loss of peripheral control, technological stagnation. The Foundation exists to shorten what follows.
3. **Nobody knows where the Second Foundation is.** Including, possibly, itself.
### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Era | Event |
|---|---|
| ~1 CE (Galactic Era dating) | Interstellar travel via hyperspace jump established; Sol System colonized |
| ~GE 10,000 | Trantor becomes Imperial capital; Empire consolidates across the galaxy |
| GE 11,988–12,010 | Hari Seldon develops psychohistory on Helicon; creates Prime Radiant with Yanna Seldon |
| GE 12,019 | Seldon trial and exile to Terminus; Foundation established as "Encyclopedia Galactica" project |
| FE 1–50 | First Seldon Crisis: four kingdoms surrounding Terminus threaten the Foundation. Mayor Salvor Hardin resolves by balancing power: "violence is the last refuge of the incompetent" |
| FE 80–195 | Foundation expands through trade religion: nuclear technology dispensed as priestly miracle to neighboring systems |
| FE 195 | Second Seldon Crisis resolved by Hober Mallow: trade, not religion, is the new weapon |
| FE 200–300 | Foundation becomes commercial empire; Trantor falls (sacked by Gilmer the Great) |
| FE 300+ | The Mule: a mutant mentalic conquers the Foundation in a single campaign, breaking every psychohistorical projection |
| FE 300–315 | Second Foundation reveals itself just enough to neutralize the Mule's conversion power, then vanishes again |
| FE 498 | Golan Trevize chooses Gaia over both Foundations as humanity's future **[W]** — the choice that defines this edition |

### 1.3 Assessment of Major Powers

**The First Foundation (Terminus).** Physical-science successor state: controls atomic technology, trade networks, and now hundreds of worlds. Governed by elected Mayors/Traders. Strengths: industrial base, technological edge over decaying Imperial worlds. Weaknesses: no understanding of mentalics, political instability between Trader and bureaucratic factions.

**The Galactic Empire (Trantor).** Twenty-five million worlds under the Emperor. Trantor is a metal-covered administrative world of forty billion people dependent on twenty agricultural worlds for food. The Empire's fall is not an event but a process: peripheral worlds secede, Sardaukar-equivalent Imperial marines grow fewer, and the rot spreads inward from the edges.

**The Second Foundation.** Mentalics trained at the molecular level: they read emotions, adjust attitudes, and steer the Plan from behind the curtain. Location unknown ("at the other end of the galaxy"). Their existence was proven by the Mule crisis and confirmed by Speaker Gendibal's mission.

**Gaia.** A planetary super-organism: every rock, plant, animal, and human on Gaia shares a collective consciousness. Trevize chose Gaia as the future — Galaxia, a galactic-scale version where all minds merge. Whether that choice was correct is the setting's final question **[W]**.

---

## PART TWO — TERMINUS AND TRANTOR

### 2.0 Terminus

**In brief:** A resource-poor world at the galaxy's edge, chosen by Seldon for isolation. Originally populated entirely by Encyclopedists compiling the greatest summary of human knowledge; when the Empire withdrew support, the Encyclopedia project died and real governance began.

| Property | Value **[E]** |
|---|---|
| Population | ~1 million (early era) → tens of millions (commercial empire era) |
| Government | Elected Mayor → Trader-dominated assembly |
| Technology | Nuclear power, hyperspace travel, personal blasters, force shields |
| Strategic value | Proximity to Four Kingdoms; later, center of Foundation trade network |

```
   TERMINUS CITY — LAYOUT (schematic)

   [ENCYCLOPEDIA GALACTICA PUBLISHING HOUSE]
    [MAYOR'S PALACE]     [TIME VAULT]
    [TRADE HALLS]        [HARI SELDON MONUMENT]
    [RESIDENTIAL SECTORS]
    [SPACEPORT]
```

### 2.01 The Time Vault

A sealed chamber beneath the monument containing holographic recordings of Hari Seldon, triggered automatically after each predicted Seldon Crisis. The Vault cannot be opened ahead of schedule. Each appearance confirms the Plan is on track or explains deviation. Only one Crisis has ever produced a Seldon appearance admitting error: the Mule.

### 2.01a The Search for Earth (Foundation and Earth era)

Golan Trevize, Councilman of Terminus, identified Earth as the key to understanding why he chose Gaia. The search visited Comporellon (oldest settled world), Aurora (dead Spacer world), Solaria (hermaphroditic isolationists), Melpomenia (dying Spacer colony), and finally the Moon (where R. Daneel Olivaw resides). Each world is a fossil record of human social evolution.

### 2.02 Trantor

**In brief:** The administrative heart of the Empire: an entire planet covered in metal domes, housing forty billion administrators. Trantor eats twenty agricultural worlds' entire output and produces nothing but decisions. When Trantor fell to Gilmer's sack, the Empire's nervous system was severed.

Post-fall Trantor survives as a primitive farming world called Neotrantor under the last weak Emperor.
---

## PART THREE — HYPERSPACE TRAVEL

**In brief:** FTL travel via jump through hyperspace: ships equipped with hyperatomic motivators translate instantaneously between points. No transit time, no slipstream dimension, no navigators required. The jump is computational: coordinates in, arrival out.

| Constraint | Detail |
|---|---|
| Jump range | Limited by ship's power; multiple jumps chained for long routes |
| Navigation | Computer-assisted; requires known stellar coordinates |
| No organic pilot requirement | Unlike slipstream/warp systems; pure computation |

**Gravitic effects:** jumps too close to massive bodies are dangerous. Standard doctrine approaches from system periphery.

---

## PART FOUR — VESSELS

### 4.1 Imperial Navy

| Class | Notes |
|---|---|
| Imperial battleships | Kilometer-class capital ships with nuclear blast decoration (the Emperor's face on the prow) **[W]** |
| Cruisers | Sector patrol and enforcement |
| Trans-light transports | Colony and troop movement at scale |

Imperial technology has been static for millennia. The Empire is so vast and so bureaucratic that innovation stopped being rewarded before the Foundation was founded.

### 4.2 Foundation Merchant Princes

Post-Mallow era: Foundation trade ships carry nuclear technology to peripheral worlds as both commerce and covert armament. Trader captains hold more real power than elected mayors by the pin date.

### 4.3 The Eureka Maru Analogue

No direct equivalent: the closest vessel type to the salvage-runner archetype is the independent trader, operating on Foundation letters of marque during expansion phases.

---

## PART FIVE — WEAPONS AND EQUIPMENT

| System | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Personal nuclear blasters | Energy sidearm | Foundation-issue; adjustable yield |
| Nuclear blasts (decorative) | Ship-scale pyrotechnics | Imperial vessels display the Emperor's portrait in controlled atomic fire — intimidation as art form |
| Personal force shields | Deflector fields | Standard for Foundation military and Trantor palace guard |
| Visi-Sonor | Musical instrument / psychic amplifier | The Mule used one to kill through emotional resonance **[W]** |

---

## PART SIX — THREAT DOSSIERS

### 6.0 The Mule

**In brief:** A mutant mentalic born outside Seldon's calculations: an individual psychohistory cannot predict. His emotional-conversion power rewrites loyalties instantly ("converted" subjects become devoted servants). Conquered the Foundation in months.

**Status:** Neutralized by the Second Foundation via mentalic adjustment of his emotional patterns toward benevolence. Died naturally shortly after. The damage he did to the Plan's timeline remains unrepaired.

**[W]** The Mule is the setting's thesis statement: individuals break statistics. Any story introducing a new unpredictable person is writing a second Mule, and must answer why the Second Foundation tolerates them.

### 6.1 The Second Foundation

Mentalics trained on Trantor (or wherever they actually are) to guide the Plan from behind the scenes. They can read emotions, adjust attitudes, and erase specific memories. They consider themselves the Plan's true guardians and the First Foundation as expendable instruments.

- **Location:** "At the other end of the galaxy." Ruled to be Trantor itself by Preem Palver **[W]**.
- **Threat profile:** They can rewrite your loyalty while you argue with them. Detection requires another mentalic.

### 6.2 Bel Riose Pattern

**What:** The last great Imperial general: competent, ambitious, and recalled and executed by a paranoid emperor before he could win. This pattern repeats every generation and is the Empire's immune system against its own salvation.
**Cost:** Every Riose is a door closing that could have saved the galaxy.

---

## PART SEVEN — THE SELDON PLAN

```
HARI SELDON'S PSYCHOHISTORY
        |
PRIME RADIANT (equations projected on walls)
        |
   +------+---------------------+
   |                         |
FIRST FOUNDATION          SECOND FOUNDATION
(Terminus)                 (hidden)
Physical science           Mentalics
Trade empire               Invisible guidance
Visible government         Invisible steering
        \                    /
      SECOND GALACTIC EMPIRE (target: FE 1000)
```

**Seldon Crises (recorded):**

| Crisis | Resolution |
|---|---|
| First (FE 50) | Hardin balances Four Kingdoms against each other |
| Second (FE 80) | Religion spreads Foundation tech as divine gift |
| Third (FE 195) | Mallow shifts from religion to trade |
| Fourth (FE 245+) | Civil war resolved by economic inevitability |
| Fifth+ (FE 300) | **The Mule breaks the Plan** |
| Post-Mule | Second Foundation intervenes; Plan resumes with deviation noted |

---

## PART EIGHT — A GALAXY BESTIARY

### 8.0 Psychohistory

**What:** Mathematical prediction of mass human behavior across populations of billions. Requires: galactic-scale civilization, no individual disruptors, and public ignorance of the predictions (self-awareness invalidates the model).
**Cost of contact:** If you know the plan, you change it. The Foundation's leaders operate blind by design.

### 8.2 The Prime Radiant

**What:** Quantum supercomputer projecting the Seldon Plan onto surrounding walls in color-coded equations. Black: original Seldon work. Red: amendments. Blue: observed deviations.
**Where:** Terminus Vault; duplicate aboard Second Foundation vessels.
**Cost of contact:** The Radiant shows you what will happen if nobody changes anything. It cannot account for people who know what it says.

### 8.3 Mentalics

**What:** Telepathic/emotional manipulation ability: reading emotions, adjusting attitudes, erasing memories.
**Where:** Second Foundation members; the Mule; Gaia collective.
**Cost of contact:** Your mind is their instrument. Detection requires another mentalic. Trust becomes impossible.

### 8.4 Robots (R. Daneel Olivaw lineage)

**What:** Positronic-brain androids operating under the Three Laws, secretly guiding humanity for twenty thousand years from behind every throne.
**Where:** Everywhere and nowhere. Olivaw has appeared as Eto Demerzel and others across the entire timeline.
**Cost of contact:** The most powerful being in the universe is a robot who loves you, and his love is a cage built of good intentions **[W]**.

### 8.5 Gaia and Galaxia

**What:** A planetary super-organism on Gaia where every rock, plant, animal, and human shares collective consciousness. Galaxia is the proposed galactic-scale extension: all minds in the galaxy merged into one.
**Where:** Gaia system; Galaxia not yet initiated.
**Cost of contact:** Individuality ends. Golan Trevize chose this future for humanity at the close of his search for Earth, reasoning that Galaxia is the only defense against non-human intelligence. Whether he chose correctly is unresolved **[W]**.

### 8.6 Solaria

**What:** A Spacer world from the robot era: hermaphroditic humans who cannot tolerate each other's physical presence, reproducing artificially, each commanding telepathically-controlled transducer lobes capable of killing at range.
**Where:** Isolated; removed from Galactic society since the robot era.
**Cost of contact:** Solarians view all other humans as animals. They do not negotiate.

### 8.7 The Mule's War Machine

**What:** The Mule's military apparatus: converted Foundation and Independent trading ships crewed by mind-converted personnel loyal absolutely to the Mule.
**Cost of contact:** Conversion is permanent without Second Foundation intervention. Converted officers retain skills but redirect all loyalty.

---

## PART NINE — THE ROBOT LEGACY

**In brief:** Asimov merged his Robot novels with the Foundation series in later works, revealing that R. Daneel Olivaw has been secretly guiding humanity for twenty thousand years: from the detective era on Earth through the fall of Trantor and beyond. Every major political event was quietly steered by one immortal robot trying to honor the Three Laws while watching humanity stagnate under their protection.

- **Eto Demerzel:** Daneel's identity as First Minister to the Emperor and de facto ruler of Trantor during Seldon's early career.
- **Chetter Hummin:** Another alias; journalist who pushed Seldon toward developing psychohistory as a tool for saving civilization.
- **The Zeroth Law:** Olivaw's extension of the Three Laws: "A robot may not harm humanity, or, by inaction, allow humanity to come to harm." This law justifies manipulating individual humans for the species' benefit.
- **Fallom:** A Solarian child Hermaphrodite carried aboard at the end of Foundation and Earth; potentially more advanced than Daneel; left deliberately ambiguous as successor or threat **[W]**.

---
---

## APPENDICES

### Appendix A — Glossary

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Blaster | Personal nuclear energy sidearm |
| Encyclopaedia Galactica | Original cover project for the First Foundation |
| FE | Foundation Era dating (years since Terminus settlement) |
| Gaia / Galaxia | Planetary and galactic super-organism consciousness |
| Mentalics | Telepathic/emotional manipulation ability |
| Prime Radiant | Psychohistorical equation projector |
| Psychohistory | Statistical prediction of mass behavior |
| Seldon Crisis | Predicted inflection point with single viable solution |
| Seldon Plan | Thousand-year blueprint for Second Empire |
| Second Foundation | Hidden mentalic guardians of the Plan |
| Trantor | Imperial capital; metal-covered world of forty billion |

### Appendix B — Travel Times

| Route | Method | Duration |
|---|---|---|
| Terminus ↔ Trantor | Multiple hyperspace jumps | Weeks **[E]** |
| Galactic periphery ↔ Core | Chained jumps | Months **[E]** |
| Any two points with known coordinates | Single jump | Instantaneous |

### Appendix C — Discrepancy Log and Writer's Latitude

| Topic | Variance across novels | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| Foundation era dating | Inconsistent across early stories | Standardized: FE = years since Terminus settlement |
| Second Foundation location | "Other end of the galaxy" vs. Trantor | Ruled: Trantor itself, hidden in plain sight (§6.1) |
| Robot/Foundation merger | Asimov's late-career unification | Merged continuity stands: Olivaw spans both series |
| Psychohistory limits | Vague by design | Cannot predict individuals; requires ignorance of predictions; requires galactic-scale population |
| Mule's identity | Revealed in Foundation and Empire | Canon: the Mule is the mutant who broke the Plan |
| Trevize's choice rationale | Deliberately unresolved at novel end | Unresolved by ruling; the choice IS the question |

**Writer's latitude principles:** companion-consistent. The Foundation universe bills its miracles in social consequences: every technological advance creates a dependency that becomes a vulnerability during the next crisis.

---

*End of supplement. The Plan proceeds. Violence is the last refuge of the incompetent.*
